**PHASE 3 -- Process Modeling & Solution Design**

**Lesson 15: Process Modeling Fundamentals**

**Understanding Processes, Workflows, AS-IS & TO-BE Analysis**

**A BA does not only define what a system should do. A BA understands
how the business works today and designs how it should work tomorrow.**

Before creating software requirements, a BA must understand the
underlying business process.

Example:

A stakeholder says:

\"We need AI automation for pharmacovigilance.\"

A weak BA asks:

\"What feature do you want?\"

A strong BA asks:

\"How does the current safety process work? Where are delays? Where can
AI improve the workflow?\"

**Learning Objectives**

By the end of this lesson, you will understand:

-   What is a business process

-   Process vs workflow

-   Process modeling concepts

-   Why BAs model processes

-   AS-IS process mapping

-   TO-BE process design

-   Process improvement approach

-   Flowcharts

-   SIPOC Model

-   Value Stream Mapping

-   Healthcare process example

**1. What is a Business Process?**

Definition:

A business process is a sequence of activities performed to achieve a
specific business outcome.

Simple:

A process describes:

**Who does what, when, and why.**

Example:

Pharmacovigilance Case Processing Process:

Receive Case

↓

Validate Information

↓

Enter Data

↓

Medical Review

↓

Quality Check

↓

Submit Report

**2. Process Components**

Every process has:

**1. Trigger**

What starts the process?

Example:

Adverse event report received.

**2. Inputs**

What information enters?

Example:

Patient report, medical documents.

**3. Activities**

Steps performed.

Example:

Review, classify, approve.

**4. Actors**

Who performs activities?

Example:

Safety reviewer.

**5. Outputs**

Final result.

Example:

Regulatory safety report.

**6. Rules**

Constraints.

Example:

Serious cases require escalation.

**Process Structure**

Trigger

↓

Input

↓

Activities

↓

Decision Points

↓

Output

**3. What is Process Modeling?**

Definition:

Process modeling is the visual representation of business processes
using diagrams, symbols, and rules.

Instead of writing:

\"Reviewer checks case, approves, then submits.\"

We draw:

Receive Case

\|

Validate Data

\|

Medical Review

\|

Approval

\|

Submission

**Why Process Modeling?**

A BA uses process models to:

✅ Understand current operations\
✅ Identify problems\
✅ Remove inefficiencies\
✅ Define automation opportunities\
✅ Communicate with stakeholders\
✅ Design improved workflows

**4. Process vs Workflow**

This is a common interview question.

**Business Process**

Higher-level view.

Focus:

Business objective.

Example:

\"Manage Adverse Event Cases.\"

**Workflow**

Detailed sequence of tasks.

Focus:

How work moves between people/systems.

Example:

Reviewer receives case

↓

AI extracts information

↓

Reviewer validates

↓

Manager approves

Difference:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Process**                         **Workflow**
  ----------------------------------- -----------------------------------
  Business view                       Operational view

  Why work happens                    How work happens

  Strategic                           Detailed

  End-to-end                          Task sequence
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**5. Process Modeling Levels**

A BA usually works at different levels.

**Level 0: Enterprise Process Map**

High-level business areas.

Example:

Pharmaceutical Company:

Drug Development

↓

Clinical Trials

↓

Regulatory Affairs

↓

Pharmacovigilance

↓

Product Support

**Level 1: Process**

Example:

Pharmacovigilance Management.

**Level 2: Sub-process**

Example:

Adverse Event Case Processing.

**Level 3: Activities**

Example:

-   Receive case

-   Validate data

-   Review case

**6. AS-IS Process Mapping**

**What is AS-IS?**

AS-IS represents:

How the process works today.

Before improvement.

Example:

Current manual PV process:

Report Received

↓

Reviewer Reads Document

↓

Manual Data Entry

↓

Manual Classification

↓

Medical Review

↓

Submission

**Why Create AS-IS?**

To identify:

-   Bottlenecks

-   Delays

-   Manual work

-   Errors

-   Duplicate activities

**AS-IS Analysis Example**

Problem:

Case processing takes 5 days.

BA investigates:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Activity**                                      **Time**
  ------------------------------------------------- ---------------------
  Document reading                                  2 hours

  Data entry                                        3 hours

  Validation                                        1 day

  Approval waiting                                  2 days
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

Finding:

Approval waiting is biggest bottleneck.

**7. TO-BE Process Design**

**What is TO-BE?**

TO-BE represents:

How the future improved process should work.

Example:

AI-enabled PV process:

Report Received

↓

AI Extracts Information

↓

AI Suggests Classification

↓

Reviewer Validates

↓

Medical Approval

↓

Submission

**AS-IS vs TO-BE**

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **AS-IS**                          **TO-BE**
  ---------------------------------- ------------------------------------
  Manual extraction                  AI extraction

  Multiple systems                   Integrated platform

  Long review time                   Faster review

  More errors                        Validation support
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**8. Process Improvement Cycle**

A BA follows:

Understand Current Process

↓

Identify Problems

↓

Analyze Root Cause

↓

Design Future Process

↓

Define Requirements

↓

Implement Solution

↓

Measure Improvement

**9. Flowcharts**

One of the simplest process modeling techniques.

Common symbols:

**Start / End**

Oval

(Start)

**Activity**

Rectangle

\[Review Case\]

**Decision**

Diamond

\<Is Case Serious?\>

**Flow**

Arrow

\-\-\--\>

Example:

(Start)

↓

Receive Report

↓

Validate Data

↓

\<Complete?\>

Yes ↓

Review Case

No ↓

Request Information

**10. SIPOC Model**

Very useful BA technique.

SIPOC:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Letter**                  **Meaning**
  --------------------------- -------------------------------------------
  S                           Supplier

  I                           Input

  P                           Process

  O                           Output

  C                           Customer
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

Example:

AI Pharmacovigilance Process:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **SIPOC**              **Example**
  ---------------------- ------------------------------------------------
  Supplier               Healthcare provider

  Input                  Adverse event report

  Process                Case processing

  Output                 Safety report

  Customer               Regulatory authority
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**When to use SIPOC?**

Use when:

-   Starting process analysis

-   Understanding scope

-   Identifying stakeholders

**11. Value Stream Mapping**

Used in Lean methodology.

Purpose:

Identify:

-   Value-added activities

-   Waste

-   Delays

Example:

Current process:

Receive Report

(Waiting 2 days)

Manual Entry

(Waiting 1 day)

Review

Waste:

Waiting time.

Future:

AI reduces waiting.

**12. Healthcare AI Example**

**Project:**

AI Pharmacovigilance Copilot

**AS-IS Process**

Patient Report

↓

Safety Team Receives Document

↓

Reviewer Reads Document

↓

Manual Data Entry

↓

Medical Review

↓

Submission

Problems:

-   Slow

-   Manual effort

-   Data errors

**TO-BE Process**

Patient Report

↓

AI Document Processing

↓

Information Extraction

↓

Reviewer Validation

↓

AI Summary Generation

↓

Medical Approval

↓

Submission

Benefits:

-   Faster processing

-   Better consistency

-   Reduced workload

**13. Process Modeling Documents Created by BA**

Common artifacts:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Document**                     **Purpose**
  -------------------------------- --------------------------------------
  Process Map                      Understand workflow

  AS-IS Diagram                    Current state

  TO-BE Diagram                    Future state

  SIPOC                            Scope understanding

  Workflow Diagram                 Task sequence

  BPMN Model                       Detailed process
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**14. Interview Questions**

**Q1: Why do BAs create process models?**

Answer:

\"Process models help BAs understand current operations, identify
improvement opportunities, communicate with stakeholders, and define
future-state solutions.\"

**Q2: Difference between AS-IS and TO-BE?**

Answer:

\"AS-IS represents the current process, while TO-BE represents the
improved future process after changes are implemented.\"

**Q3: What is SIPOC?**

Answer:

\"SIPOC is a high-level process analysis technique that identifies
Suppliers, Inputs, Process, Outputs, and Customers.\"

**Practical Assignment**

Create:

**AS-IS Process Map**

For:

\"Current Pharmacovigilance Case Processing\"

Include:

-   Trigger

-   Actors

-   Activities

-   Problems

**TO-BE Process Map**

After implementing:

\"AI Pharmacovigilance Copilot\"

Include:

-   AI activities

-   Human activities

-   Decision points

-   Improvements

**Lesson Summary**

You learned:

✅ Business process concepts\
✅ Process modeling\
✅ Process vs workflow\
✅ AS-IS mapping\
✅ TO-BE design\
✅ Flowcharts\
✅ SIPOC\
✅ Value Stream Mapping\
✅ Healthcare AI process example

**PHASE 3 -- Process Modeling & Solution Design**

**Lesson 16: BPMN 2.0 Fundamentals**

**Business Process Model and Notation for Professional BAs**

**BPMN is the language that allows business people, BAs, developers,
architects, and process teams to understand the same business process
visually.**

A senior BA is expected to convert business workflows into standardized
process diagrams.

**Learning Objectives**

By the end of this lesson, you will understand:

-   What BPMN is

-   Why BAs use BPMN

-   BPMN vs Flowcharts

-   Core BPMN elements

-   Events

-   Activities

-   Gateways

-   Pools and Lanes

-   Sequence Flow

-   Message Flow

-   Basic BPMN modeling rules

-   Healthcare AI BPMN example

**1. What is BPMN?**

BPMN stands for:

**Business Process Model and Notation**

Definition:

BPMN is a standardized graphical notation used to represent business
processes and workflows.

The current widely used standard is managed by the Object Management
Group.

Simple meaning:

BPMN is a visual language for showing:

-   Who performs an activity

-   What happens next

-   Where decisions occur

-   How systems communicate

**2. Why BAs Use BPMN**

A BA works between:

-   Business stakeholders

-   Product teams

-   Developers

-   QA teams

-   Compliance teams

Each group thinks differently.

BPMN creates a common language.

Example:

Business says:

\"The reviewer checks cases and sends approval.\"

Developer needs:

-   Who checks?

-   When?

-   What conditions?

-   What happens if rejected?

BPMN answers these questions.

**3. BPMN vs Flowchart**

Both represent processes, but BPMN is more powerful.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Flowchart**                  **BPMN**
  ------------------------------ ----------------------------------------
  Simple diagrams                Industry standard

  Basic steps                    Detailed business logic

  Limited symbols                Rich notation

  Small processes                Enterprise processes
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

Example:

Flowchart:

Receive Case

\|

Review

\|

Approve

BPMN:

Patient → System → Reviewer → Manager → Regulatory Team

with:

\- events

\- decisions

\- messages

\- responsibilities

**4. BPMN Core Elements**

BPMN has four main categories:

1\. Flow Objects

2\. Connecting Objects

3\. Swimlanes

4\. Artifacts

**1. Flow Objects**

These represent what happens in a process.

Three main types:

1.  Events

2.  Activities

3.  Gateways

**5. Events**

Events represent something that happens.

Usually drawn as circles.

There are three types:

**A. Start Event**

Shows where the process begins.

Symbol:

○

Example:

\"Adverse Event Report Received\"

**B. Intermediate Event**

Something happens during the process.

Example:

\"Timer Waiting\"

\"Approval Received\"

**C. End Event**

Shows process completion.

Symbol:

◎

Example:

\"Safety Report Submitted\"

**BPMN Event Flow**

(Start)

↓

Process Activities

↓

(End)

**6. Activities**

Activities represent work performed.

Symbol:

Rounded rectangle.

\[ Activity \]

Two types:

**Task**

Single activity.

Example:

\[Review Case\]

**Sub-Process**

A group of activities.

Example:

\[Process Safety Case\]

contains:

\- Extract Data

\- Validate Data

\- Classify Event

**7. Gateways**

Gateways represent decisions.

Symbol:

Diamond.

◇

Example:

Decision:

\"Is case serious?\"

◇

Serious?

/ \\

Yes No

**Types of Gateways**

**1. Exclusive Gateway (XOR)**

Only one path selected.

Example:

If serious:

Escalate

Else:

Normal processing

Symbol:

◇ with X

**2. Parallel Gateway (AND)**

Multiple paths happen together.

Example:

After case validation:

-   Medical review

-   Data quality check

Both happen.

Symbol:

◇ with +

**3. Inclusive Gateway (OR)**

One or more paths possible.

**8. Connecting Objects**

They show relationships.

**Sequence Flow**

Shows order of activities.

Symbol:

Solid arrow.

Example:

Task A → Task B

Used inside the same process.

**Message Flow**

Shows communication between different participants.

Symbol:

Dashed arrow.

Example:

Pharma Company sends report to Regulatory Authority.

Company

\|

\| - - - \>

\|

Regulator

**Association**

Connects notes or information.

**9. Swimlanes: Pools and Lanes**

Very important BPMN concept.

Used to show responsibility.

**Pool**

Represents a participant.

Example:

A company.

\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--

Pharma Company

\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--

**Lane**

Represents roles/departments inside a pool.

Example:

\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--

Pharma Company

Safety Reviewer

Medical Reviewer

Regulatory Team

\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--

**Example:**

AI Pharmacovigilance BPMN Participants:

Pool:

Pharmaceutical Company

Lanes:

1.  Safety Reviewer

2.  AI System

3.  Medical Reviewer

4.  Regulatory Team

**10. BPMN Modeling Rules**

Important rules:

**Rule 1**

Every process needs:

Start Event

and

End Event

**Rule 2**

Sequence flow connects activities.

**Rule 3**

Use gateways for decisions.

**Rule 4**

Use lanes to show responsibility.

**Rule 5**

Do not mix business process and technical details unnecessarily.

**11. Basic BPMN Example**

**Process:**

AI Adverse Event Processing

START

↓

\[Receive Report\]

↓

\[AI Extract Information\]

↓

◇ Complete Data?

↓Yes

\[Reviewer Validation\]

↓

◇ Serious Case?

↓Yes

\[Medical Review\]

↓

\[Regulatory Submission\]

↓

END

**12. Healthcare AI BPMN Design**

**Pool:**

Pharmaceutical Company

**Lane 1:**

Safety Reviewer

Activities:

-   Receive case

-   Validate AI output

**Lane 2:**

AI System

Activities:

-   Extract information

-   Generate summary

**Lane 3:**

Medical Reviewer

Activities:

-   Clinical assessment

**Lane 4:**

Regulatory Team

Activities:

-   Submit report

Visual logic:

Safety Reviewer

(Start)

\|

Receive Case

\|

AI System

\|

Extract Data

\|

Generate Summary

\|

Safety Reviewer

\|

Validate

\|

Medical Reviewer

\|

Approve

\|

Regulatory

\|

Submit

(End)

**13. BPMN Tools Used by BAs**

Common tools:

-   Camunda Modeler

-   Signavio

-   Microsoft Visio

-   Lucid Software

**14. Interview Questions**

**Q1: What is BPMN?**

Answer:

\"BPMN is a standardized notation used to visually model business
processes, showing activities, events, decisions, responsibilities, and
communication flows.\"

**Q2: Difference between BPMN and Flowchart?**

Answer:

\"Flowcharts provide simple process visualization, while BPMN provides a
standardized enterprise-level notation with concepts like events,
gateways, pools, and message flows.\"

**Q3: What are BPMN swimlanes?**

Answer:

\"Swimlanes, consisting of pools and lanes, represent participants and
responsibilities involved in a process.\"

**Practical Assignment**

Create a BPMN Level-1 process for:

**AI Pharmacovigilance Case Processing**

Include:

**Pool:**

Pharmaceutical Company

**Lanes:**

1.  Safety Reviewer

2.  AI System

3.  Medical Reviewer

4.  Regulatory Team

Include:

-   Start Event

-   End Event

-   Minimum 8 Activities

-   One Exclusive Gateway

-   One Message Flow

**Lesson Summary**

You learned:

✅ BPMN purpose\
✅ BPMN vs Flowcharts\
✅ Events\
✅ Activities\
✅ Gateways\
✅ Pools and Lanes\
✅ Sequence Flow\
✅ Message Flow\
✅ BPMN modeling rules\
✅ Healthcare AI example

**Lesson 17: Advanced BPMN Modeling**

**From Basic Workflow to Enterprise-Level Process Design**

**Basic BPMN shows what happens. Advanced BPMN explains exceptions,
timing, failures, parallel activities, and complex business decisions.**

In real organizations, processes are rarely simple.

Example:

A pharmacovigilance process is not:

Receive Case → Review → Submit

Real life:

-   What if documents are incomplete?

-   What if reviewer rejects the case?

-   What if approval is delayed?

-   What if AI extraction fails?

-   What if multiple teams work simultaneously?

Advanced BPMN handles these situations.

**Learning Objectives**

By the end of this lesson, you will understand:

-   Intermediate Events

-   Timer Events

-   Error Events

-   Boundary Events

-   Message Events

-   Parallel Processing

-   Event-Based Gateway

-   Sub-Processes

-   Call Activities

-   BPMN best practices

-   Enterprise healthcare AI workflow

**1. BPMN Advanced Elements Overview**

Basic BPMN:

Start

\|

Task

\|

Decision

\|

End

Advanced BPMN adds:

Events

↓

Exceptions

↓

Parallel activities

↓

Reusable processes

↓

System communication

**2. Intermediate Events**

**What are Intermediate Events?**

An event that occurs **between start and end** of a process.

It represents something happening during execution.

Example:

Pharmacovigilance workflow:

Start

↓

Receive Case

↓

(wait for additional documents)

↓

Review Case

↓

End

The waiting step is an intermediate event.

**Types of Intermediate Events**

**1. Timer Event**

**2. Message Event**

**3. Error Event**

**4. Signal Event**

**3. Timer Events**

A timer event represents time-based conditions.

Symbol:

Circle with clock.

Example:

Regulatory rule:

\"Serious adverse events must be submitted within 15 days.\"

BPMN:

Case Approved

↓

Timer Event

(15 Days)

↓

Submit Report

Use cases:

-   SLA deadlines

-   Reminders

-   Escalations

-   Scheduled activities

**Healthcare Example:**

If medical reviewer does not approve within 24 hours:

System sends reminder.

Process:

Waiting for Approval

↓

Timer (24 hours)

↓

Send Reminder

**4. Message Events**

Used when communication happens between participants.

Example:

Pharmaceutical Company sends report to Regulatory Authority.

BPMN:

Company Pool

\[Prepare Report\]

\|

\| - - - - \>

\|

Regulatory Authority Pool

\[Receive Report\]

Important:

Sequence Flow:

Inside same process.

Message Flow:

Between different participants.

**5. Error Events**

Used when something goes wrong.

Example:

AI extraction fails.

Normal flow:

Upload Document

↓

AI Extraction

↓

Generate Summary

Error scenario:

Upload Document

↓

AI Extraction

X

Extraction Failed

↓

Manual Review

**6. Boundary Events**

Very important BPMN concept.

A boundary event is attached to an activity.

It represents an exception during that activity.

Example:

Activity:

\"AI Processing\"

Possible problem:

Processing timeout.

BPMN:

+\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--+

\| AI Processing \|

\| \|

\| ◯ Timer \|

+\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--+

↓

Manual Review

Meaning:

If AI takes too long:

Move to manual review.

**Boundary Event Types**

**Interrupting Boundary Event**

Stops current activity.

Example:

AI failure → Manual process.

**Non-interrupting Boundary Event**

Allows activity to continue.

Example:

AI processing continues while notification is sent.

**7. Parallel Gateway**

Used when multiple activities happen simultaneously.

Symbol:

Diamond with +

Example:

After case validation:

Two teams work together:

-   Medical Review

-   Data Quality Check

Process:

\+

\|

\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--

\| \|

Medical Review Data Check

\| \|

\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--

\+

Continue

Healthcare Example:

Case review requires:

Medical assessment + Regulatory verification.

Both happen together.

**8. Event-Based Gateway**

Used when the next action depends on an event.

Symbol:

Diamond with event marker.

Example:

After submitting approval request:

Possible outcomes:

1.  Manager approves

2.  Manager rejects

3.  Timeout occurs

Process:

◇

Approval Waiting

/ \| \\

Approve Reject Timeout

**9. Sub-Processes**

A sub-process groups multiple activities.

Why?

To simplify large diagrams.

Example:

Instead of:

Case Processing

\- Extract data

\- Validate fields

\- Check duplicates

\- Classify event

\- Generate narrative

Create:

\[AI Case Processing Sub-process\]

Inside:

Upload

↓

Extract

↓

Validate

↓

Classify

↓

Summarize

**10. Call Activity**

Used when a reusable process is called from multiple places.

Example:

\"User Authentication\"

Used in:

-   Case Review

-   Reporting

-   Dashboard

Instead of creating again:

Call existing process.

**Sub-process vs Call Activity**

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Sub-process**                   **Call Activity**
  --------------------------------- -------------------------------------
  Local process                     Reusable process

  Used once                         Used multiple times

  Inside same workflow              Shared across workflows
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**11. Complete AI Pharmacovigilance BPMN Level-2 Example**

**Pool:**

Pharmaceutical Company

**Lane: Safety Reviewer**

(Start)

↓

Receive Case

↓

Upload Document

**Lane: AI System**

AI Extraction

↓

AI Classification

↓

AI Summary Generation

Decision:

Is AI Confidence \> 90%?

YES:

Reviewer Validation

NO:

Manual Review

Parallel:

\+

\|

Medical Review

Regulatory Check

\|

\+

Timer:

Approval pending

↓

24 hour timer

↓

Escalation

End:

Report Submitted

(End)

**12. BPMN Best Practices**

**Practice 1:**

Always start with business objective.

Do not immediately draw technical workflow.

**Practice 2:**

Keep diagrams readable.

Avoid:

-   Too many crossings

-   Too many gateways

**Practice 3:**

Use proper naming.

Bad:

\"Process Data\"

Good:

\"Extract Patient Information\"

**Practice 4:**

Separate:

Business activities

from

System activities.

**Practice 5:**

Model exceptions.

Real processes include failures.

**13. BPMN in BA Interviews**

**Q1: What is a boundary event?**

Answer:

\"A boundary event is an event attached to an activity that handles
exceptions or additional triggers while the activity is running.\"

**Q2: Difference between sequence flow and message flow?**

Answer:

\"Sequence flow shows order of activities inside a process, while
message flow represents communication between different participants.\"

**Q3: Why use subprocesses?**

Answer:

\"Subprocesses simplify complex processes by grouping related activities
into manageable sections.\"

**Practical Assignment**

Design Level-2 BPMN for:

**AI Pharmacovigilance Copilot**

Include:

**Events:**

✅ Start Event\
✅ Timer Event\
✅ Error Event

**Gateways:**

✅ Exclusive Gateway\
✅ Parallel Gateway

**BPMN Structure:**

Pool:

Pharmaceutical Company

Lanes:

1.  Safety Reviewer

2.  AI Engine

3.  Medical Reviewer

4.  Regulatory Team

**Lesson Summary**

You learned:

✅ Intermediate Events\
✅ Timer Events\
✅ Message Events\
✅ Error Events\
✅ Boundary Events\
✅ Parallel Gateway\
✅ Event-Based Gateway\
✅ Sub-processes\
✅ Call Activities\
✅ BPMN best practices\
✅ Enterprise workflow modeling

**Lesson 18: UML for Business Analysts**

**Understanding System Behavior Through UML Diagrams**

**BPMN explains how the business works. UML explains how the system
behaves.**

A professional BA often works between business processes and software
solutions.

Example:

Business says:

\"We need an AI pharmacovigilance platform.\"

BPMN answers:

\"How does the safety workflow operate?\"

UML answers:

\"How should users interact with the system?\"

**Learning Objectives**

By the end of this lesson, you will understand:

-   What UML is

-   Why BAs use UML

-   BPMN vs UML

-   UML diagram categories

-   Use Case Diagram

-   Activity Diagram

-   Sequence Diagram

-   Class Diagram basics

-   State Diagram

-   Healthcare AI examples

**1. What is UML?**

UML stands for:

**Unified Modeling Language**

Definition:

UML is a standardized visual modeling language used to describe software
systems, their structure, behavior, and interactions.

Simple meaning:

UML helps describe:

-   Users

-   System functions

-   Data objects

-   System interactions

-   Object behavior

**2. Why Does a BA Need UML?**

A BA does not usually write code.

But a BA must communicate with:

-   Developers

-   Architects

-   QA Engineers

-   Product Teams

UML helps translate:

Business requirement

↓

System understanding

↓

Development solution

Example:

Business Requirement:

\"Reviewer should approve AI-generated case summaries.\"

Developer needs:

-   Who approves?

-   What happens before approval?

-   What data is involved?

-   What system response occurs?

UML helps define this.

**3. BPMN vs UML**

Very important interview question.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **BPMN**                         **UML**
  -------------------------------- --------------------------------------
  Business process modeling        Software/system modeling

  Focus on workflow                Focus on system behavior

  Shows activities                 Shows objects and interactions

  Business users understand        Technical teams understand
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

Example:

**BPMN:**

\"How a safety case moves through the organization.\"

**UML:**

\"How reviewer interacts with AI system.\"

**4. UML Diagram Categories**

UML has many diagrams.

For BAs, the most important are:

**Behavioral Diagrams**

Show what the system does.

Examples:

1.  Use Case Diagram

2.  Activity Diagram

3.  Sequence Diagram

4.  State Diagram

**Structural Diagrams**

Show system structure.

Examples:

1.  Class Diagram

2.  Component Diagram

**BA Most Important UML Diagrams**

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Diagram**      **Purpose**
  ---------------- ------------------------------------------------------
  Use Case         User-system interaction

  Activity         Workflow behavior

  Sequence         Communication between objects

  Class            Data structure

  State            Object lifecycle
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**5. Use Case Diagram**

**Most important UML diagram for BA.**

Definition:

A Use Case Diagram shows how users interact with a system and what
functionality the system provides.

It contains:

**Actor**

Who uses the system.

Example:

-   Safety Reviewer

-   Medical Reviewer

-   Administrator

**Use Case**

System functionality.

Example:

-   Upload Document

-   Generate Summary

-   Approve Case

**Use Case Diagram Example**

System:

AI Pharmacovigilance Platform

Safety Reviewer

\|

\|

\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--

\| AI PV System \|

\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--

\|

\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--

\| \|

Upload Document Generate Summary

\|

Approve Case

**Actors:**

External users or systems.

Examples:

-   Reviewer

-   Regulatory Authority

-   Drug Database

**Use Cases:**

Actions performed by system.

Examples:

-   Login

-   Upload case

-   Review AI output

-   Submit report

**6. Use Case Relationship**

**Include Relationship**

Means:

One use case always uses another.

Example:

Login required before:

-   Review Case

-   Submit Report

Review Case

\|

\<\<include\>\>

\|

Login

**Extend Relationship**

Optional behavior.

Example:

Normal review:

Additional:

AI suggestion warning

Review Case

\|

\<\<extend\>\>

\|

AI Warning

**7. Writing Use Cases**

A BA creates detailed use case specifications.

Example:

**Use Case: Generate AI Summary**

**Use Case ID**

UC-001

**Actor**

Safety Reviewer

**Goal**

Generate case summary using AI.

**Preconditions**

-   User logged in

-   Document uploaded

**Main Flow**

1.  User selects Generate Summary

2.  System extracts information

3.  AI generates summary

4.  User reviews output

**Alternative Flow**

AI confidence is low:

System requests manual review.

**Post Condition**

Summary stored.

**8. Activity Diagram**

Activity Diagram represents workflow behavior.

Similar to BPMN but UML style.

Example:

AI Summary Generation:

Start

↓

Upload Document

↓

Extract Data

↓

Generate Summary

↓

Review

↓

Approve

↓

End

Used for:

-   Business workflows

-   Algorithm flows

-   User actions

**BPMN Activity vs UML Activity Diagram**

They look similar.

Difference:

BPMN:

Business process focus.

UML Activity:

System behavior focus.

**9. Sequence Diagram**

Very important for BA.

Definition:

A sequence diagram shows how different objects or systems communicate
over time.

Example:

AI Summary Generation:

Objects:

1.  Reviewer

2.  Application

3.  AI Engine

4.  Database

Sequence:

Reviewer

\|

\| Request Summary

↓

Application

\|

\| Send Document

↓

AI Engine

\|

\| Extract Data

↓

Database

\|

\| Return Result

↓

Application

\|

\| Display Summary

↓

Reviewer

Sequence diagrams answer:

-   Who talks to whom?

-   In what order?

-   What data moves?

**10. Class Diagram Basics**

Class Diagram shows data structure.

A class contains:

-   Attributes

-   Methods

Example:

Class:

Patient Case

\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--

PatientCase

Attributes:

caseID

patientAge

drugName

eventType

Methods:

validate()

submit()

\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--

Healthcare AI Example:

Classes:

-   Patient

-   Drug

-   AdverseEvent

-   CaseReport

-   Reviewer

**11. State Diagram**

Shows how an object changes state.

Example:

Adverse Event Case Lifecycle:

New

↓

Under Review

↓

Validated

↓

Approved

↓

Submitted

Possible rejection:

Under Review

↓

Rejected

↓

Rework

Used for:

-   Workflow states

-   Status transitions

-   Approval processes

**12. Complete Healthcare AI Example**

**Requirement:**

\"Reviewer should process AI-assisted safety cases.\"

**BPMN:**

Shows:

Case received → AI processing → Review → Approval

**Use Case Diagram:**

Shows:

Reviewer uses:

-   Upload Case

-   Generate Summary

-   Approve Case

**Sequence Diagram:**

Shows:

Reviewer → System → AI → Database

**State Diagram:**

Shows:

Case status:

New → Review → Approved

Together:

Business Process

↓

System Requirements

↓

UML Models

↓

Development

**13. UML Tools Used by BAs**

Common tools:

-   Lucid Software

-   Microsoft Visio

-   Visual Paradigm

-   Sparx Systems

**14. Interview Questions**

**Q1: Why does a BA use UML?**

Answer:

\"UML helps BAs visualize system behavior, clarify requirements, improve
communication with technical teams, and reduce ambiguity.\"

**Q2: Difference between BPMN and UML?**

Answer:

\"BPMN focuses on business processes and workflows, while UML focuses on
software system structure and behavior.\"

**Q3: What is a Use Case Diagram?**

Answer:

\"A Use Case Diagram represents interactions between actors and system
functionalities.\"

**Practical Assignment**

For AI Pharmacovigilance Copilot:

Create:

**1. Use Case Diagram**

Actors:

-   Safety Reviewer

-   Medical Reviewer

-   Administrator

-   Regulatory Authority

Use Cases:

-   Login

-   Upload Case

-   AI Extraction

-   Generate Summary

-   Review Case

-   Approve Case

-   Submit Report

**2. State Diagram**

For:

\"Adverse Event Case\"

States:

-   New

-   Processing

-   Review

-   Approved

-   Rejected

-   Submitted

**Lesson Summary**

You learned:

✅ UML purpose\
✅ BPMN vs UML\
✅ UML diagram types\
✅ Use Case Diagram\
✅ Activity Diagram\
✅ Sequence Diagram\
✅ Class Diagram\
✅ State Diagram\
✅ Healthcare AI application

**PHASE 3 -- Process Modeling & Solution Design**

**Lesson 19: Workflow Design & User Journey Mapping**

**Connecting Business Process, User Experience, and Product Design**

**A BA does not only design how the company works. A BA also understands
how users experience the product.**

A successful product requires both:

1.  **Efficient internal workflow**

2.  **Excellent user experience**

Example:

A pharmaceutical company may have a perfect internal process, but if
safety reviewers find the AI platform difficult to use, adoption will
fail.

**Learning Objectives**

By the end of this lesson, you will understand:

-   Difference between Process, Workflow, User Flow, and User Journey

-   Workflow design principles

-   End-to-end workflow modeling

-   User journey mapping

-   Customer Journey vs User Journey

-   Personas

-   Touchpoints

-   Pain points

-   Opportunities

-   Healthcare AI examples

**1. Process vs Workflow vs User Flow vs User Journey**

This is a very important BA/Product interview topic.

**1. Business Process**

**Question:**

**Why does the business perform this activity?**

Focus:

Business objective.

Example:

\"Pharmacovigilance Case Management\"

**2. Workflow**

**Question:**

**How does work move between people and systems?**

Focus:

Operational steps.

Example:

Case Received

↓

AI Processing

↓

Reviewer Validation

↓

Medical Approval

↓

Submission

**3. User Flow**

**Question:**

**What steps does a user take inside the product?**

Focus:

User interaction with software.

Example:

Reviewer:

Login

↓

Open Dashboard

↓

Select Case

↓

Review AI Summary

↓

Approve

**4. User Journey**

**Question:**

**What is the complete user experience before, during, and after using
the product?**

Focus:

User emotions, goals, problems.

Example:

Reviewer journey:

Receives case

↓

Feels overloaded

↓

Uses AI tool

↓

Reviews summary

↓

Feels confident

**Comparison Table**

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Concept**        **Focus**                  **Example**
  ------------------ -------------------------- -------------------------
  Process            Business goal              Case management

  Workflow           Work movement              Review approval

  User Flow          Product interaction        Screen navigation

  User Journey       Experience                 User feelings
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**2. What is Workflow Design?**

Definition:

Workflow design is the process of designing the sequence of activities,
decisions, responsibilities, and system interactions required to
complete a task.

A BA designs workflows to answer:

-   Who performs the task?

-   What happens next?

-   What decisions exist?

-   Where can automation help?

-   Where are bottlenecks?

**3. Workflow Design Principles**

**Principle 1: Clear Ownership**

Every activity should have an owner.

Bad:

\"Review case\"

Who reviews?

Better:

\"Safety reviewer validates extracted data.\"

**Principle 2: Minimize Manual Work**

Identify automation opportunities.

Example:

Manual:

Copy patient details.

Improvement:

AI extraction.

**Principle 3: Design Exception Handling**

Real processes fail.

Example:

Missing information.

Workflow should define:

\"If data missing → request additional information.\"

**Principle 4: Reduce Unnecessary Steps**

Example:

Old:

System A

↓

Manual Copy

↓

System B

Better:

System Integration

**4. Workflow Design Example**

**AI Pharmacovigilance Copilot**

**Current Workflow**

Receive Report

↓

Reviewer Reads Document

↓

Manual Data Entry

↓

Medical Review

↓

Approval

↓

Submission

Problems:

❌ Slow\
❌ Manual effort\
❌ Human errors

**Future Workflow**

Receive Report

↓

AI Extracts Information

↓

AI Generates Summary

↓

Reviewer Validates

↓

Medical Review

↓

Submission

Improvements:

✅ Faster processing\
✅ Better consistency\
✅ Reduced workload

**5. What is User Journey Mapping?**

Definition:

User Journey Mapping is a visual representation of a user\'s experience
while achieving a goal using a product or service.

A journey map shows:

-   User actions

-   Thoughts

-   Feelings

-   Problems

-   Opportunities

**6. Components of User Journey Map**

A typical journey map contains:

**1. Persona**

Who is the user?

Example:

**Persona:**

Name:

Dr. Sharma

Role:

Pharmacovigilance Reviewer

Goal:

Process safety cases faster.

**2. Scenario**

What situation?

Example:

Reviewing adverse event reports.

**3. Stages**

Major journey steps.

Example:

1.  Receive Case

2.  Analyze Information

3.  Review AI Output

4.  Approve

5.  Submit

**4. Actions**

What user does.

**5. Thoughts**

What user thinks.

Example:

\"Is this AI suggestion accurate?\"

**6. Emotions**

How user feels.

Example:

Before AI:

😟 Overwhelmed

After AI:

😊 Confident

**7. Pain Points**

Problems.

Example:

-   Too much manual reading

-   Missing information

-   Multiple systems

**8. Opportunities**

Possible improvements.

Example:

-   AI summary

-   Smart alerts

-   Auto extraction

**7. User Journey Map Example**

**Persona:**

Safety Reviewer

**Goal:**

Complete adverse event review.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Stage**       **Action**           **Pain Point** **Opportunity**
  --------------- -------------------- -------------- -------------------
  Receive Case    Opens report         Too many cases AI prioritization

  Read Documents  Manually extracts    Time consuming AI extraction
                  data                                

  Review          Checks information   Missing        AI validation
                                       details        

  Approval        Completes review     Waiting time   Workflow automation
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**8. Customer Journey vs User Journey**

Another important interview question.

**Customer Journey**

Broader experience with company.

Example:

Patient:

Search medicine

↓

Buy medicine

↓

Experience side effects

↓

Contact company

**User Journey**

Experience while using a specific product.

Example:

Safety reviewer using AI platform:

Login

↓

Review case

↓

Approve report

Difference:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Customer Journey**                 **User Journey**
  ------------------------------------ ----------------------------------
  Entire relationship                  Specific product usage

  Marketing/service focus              Product usability focus

  Customer experience                  User interaction
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**9. Personas in BA**

A persona is:

A fictional representation of a real user group based on research.

Example:

**Persona 1**

**Safety Reviewer**

Goals:

-   Process cases quickly

-   Reduce errors

Challenges:

-   High workload

-   Manual documentation

**Persona 2**

**Medical Reviewer**

Goals:

-   Make accurate clinical decisions

Challenges:

-   Missing information

**10. Touchpoints**

Touchpoint:

Any interaction between user and product/service.

AI Pharmacovigilance Example:

Touchpoints:

1.  Login screen

2.  Dashboard

3.  Case upload

4.  AI summary screen

5.  Approval page

6.  Notification

**11. Pain Point Analysis**

BA identifies:

**Current Problem**

↓

**Root Cause**

↓

**Improvement Opportunity**

Example:

Pain:

Reviewers spend 30 minutes per case.

Root Cause:

Manual extraction.

Opportunity:

AI extraction.

**12. User Journey + AI Product Example**

**Product:**

AI Pharmacovigilance Copilot

Persona:

Safety Reviewer

Journey:

**Stage 1: Receive Case**

Action:

Open new case.

Pain:

High workload.

Opportunity:

AI prioritization.

**Stage 2: Analyze Document**

Action:

Read reports.

Pain:

Large documents.

Opportunity:

AI extraction.

**Stage 3: Review AI Output**

Action:

Validate suggestions.

Pain:

Trust issues.

Opportunity:

Explainable AI.

**Stage 4: Approval**

Action:

Approve case.

Pain:

Compliance concerns.

Opportunity:

Audit trail.

**13. Workflow vs User Journey Relationship**

They work together.

Example:

Workflow:

AI extracts data

↓

Reviewer validates

User Journey:

Reviewer feels relieved because manual entry is reduced.

**14. BA Interview Questions**

**Q1: Difference between workflow and user journey?**

Answer:

\"Workflow focuses on the sequence of activities required to complete a
business task, while user journey focuses on the user\'s experience,
emotions, and interactions throughout that process.\"

**Q2: Why create user journey maps?**

Answer:

\"User journey maps help identify user needs, pain points,
opportunities, and improve product experiences.\"

**Q3: What is a persona?**

Answer:

\"A persona is a research-based representation of a target user group
used to guide product and requirement decisions.\"

**Practical Assignment**

Create a User Journey Map for:

**AI Pharmacovigilance Copilot**

Persona:

Safety Reviewer

Create:

1.  User Goal

2.  Journey Stages

3.  User Actions

4.  Thoughts

5.  Pain Points

6.  Opportunities

Minimum:

5 journey stages.

**Lesson Summary**

You learned:

✅ Process vs Workflow vs User Flow vs User Journey\
✅ Workflow design\
✅ User Journey Mapping\
✅ Customer Journey vs User Journey\
✅ Personas\
✅ Touchpoints\
✅ Pain points\
✅ Healthcare AI journey example

**Lesson 20: Wireframing & Prototyping for Business Analysts**

**Translating Requirements into Product Experiences**

**A BA does not only describe what the system should do. A great BA
helps stakeholders visualize how the solution will work.**

Many requirement problems happen because people interpret written
requirements differently.

Example:

Requirement:

\"Create an AI dashboard for safety reviewers.\"

Different people imagine different things:

Business thinks:

-   KPI dashboard

Developer thinks:

-   Data tables

User thinks:

-   Easy case review screen

A wireframe removes ambiguity.

**Learning Objectives**

By the end of this lesson, you will understand:

-   Why BAs need wireframing skills

-   Difference between wireframe and prototype

-   Low, medium, and high fidelity designs

-   Screen design principles

-   User flow to wireframe conversion

-   Prototype validation

-   Healthcare AI example

**1. What is Wireframing?**

Definition:

A wireframe is a visual blueprint of a product interface that shows
screen structure, layout, navigation, and functionality.

Simple:

A wireframe is like an architectural drawing of a house before
construction.

Example:

Before building:

\"AI Case Review Screen\"

BA creates:

\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--

Case ID: AE-10234

Patient Information

\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--

AI Summary

\[Generated Summary\]

\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--

Risk Level: HIGH

\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--

\[Approve\] \[Reject\]

\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--

**2. Why BAs Need Wireframing**

A BA uses wireframes to:

✅ Clarify requirements\
✅ Validate user expectations\
✅ Identify missing functionality\
✅ Improve stakeholder communication\
✅ Reduce development rework

Example:

Requirement:

\"User should review AI output.\"

Questions appear:

-   Where is AI output shown?

-   Can user edit it?

-   Can user approve?

-   Can user compare original vs AI?

Wireframe answers these.

**3. Wireframe vs Mockup vs Prototype**

Very common interview topic.

**Wireframe**

Focus:

Structure and layout.

Usually:

-   Black and white

-   Simple blocks

-   No detailed styling

Example:

Header

Menu

Content Area

Button

**Mockup**

Focus:

Visual appearance.

Includes:

-   Colors

-   Fonts

-   Images

-   Branding

**Prototype**

Focus:

Interaction.

Shows:

-   Clicks

-   Navigation

-   User flow

Example:

Click:

\"Generate Summary\"

↓

Moves to summary screen.

Comparison:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
                    **Wireframe**     **Mockup**         **Prototype**
  ----------------- ----------------- ------------------ ----------------
  Structure         ✅                ✅                 ✅

  Design            ❌                ✅                 ✅

  Interaction       ❌                ❌/Limited         ✅
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**4. Fidelity Levels**

Fidelity means:

\"How close is the design to the final product?\"

**Level 1: Low Fidelity Wireframe**

Simple sketch.

Purpose:

Early discussion.

Example:

+\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--+

\| Dashboard \|

+\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--+

\| Cases \|

\| Button \|

Used during:

Requirement discovery.

**Level 2: Medium Fidelity**

More detail.

Includes:

-   Real fields

-   Navigation

-   Components

Example:

Case Dashboard

Search Case \[\_\_\_\_\]

Filters:

Status

Priority

Date

Case List

**Level 3: High Fidelity**

Almost final design.

Includes:

-   Colors

-   Branding

-   Real interactions

Used for:

User testing.

**5. Wireframe Creation Process**

A BA follows:

Understand Requirement

↓

Identify User Goal

↓

Create User Flow

↓

Design Screens

↓

Review With Stakeholders

↓

Improve

**6. From User Story to Wireframe**

Example User Story:

As a safety reviewer, I want AI-generated summaries so that I can review
cases faster.

Convert into screens:

**Screen 1:**

Login

↓

**Screen 2:**

Case Dashboard

↓

**Screen 3:**

Case Details

↓

**Screen 4:**

AI Summary Review

↓

**Screen 5:**

Approval

**7. AI Pharmacovigilance Wireframe Example**

**Screen: Case Review Dashboard**

\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--

AI Pharmacovigilance Copilot

Search Case: \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_

Filters:

\[High Risk\] \[Pending\] \[Approved\]

Case List

\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--

Case ID Drug Risk Status

AE-001 Drug A High Review

AE-002 Drug B Low Approved

\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--

**Screen: AI Summary Review**

\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--

Case ID: AE-001

Patient Details

Age:

Gender:

History:

AI Generated Summary

\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--

Patient experienced headache

after medication administration.

Confidence Score:

92%

\[Edit\] \[Approve\]

\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--

**8. Important BA Wireframe Components**

A BA should understand:

**Navigation**

How users move.

Example:

Dashboard → Case → Review → Approval

**Information Architecture**

How information is organized.

Example:

Case information:

-   Patient details

-   Drug information

-   Event details

**User Actions**

What users can do.

Example:

-   Upload

-   Edit

-   Approve

-   Reject

**Validation Messages**

Example:

\"Required field missing.\"

**Error Handling**

Example:

\"AI extraction failed. Manual entry required.\"

**9. Prototype**

Definition:

A prototype is an interactive representation of a product used to test
ideas and collect feedback before development.

Example:

Stakeholder clicks:

Upload Document

↓

AI Processing

↓

Summary Generated

↓

Approve

No actual backend exists.

It demonstrates experience.

**10. Why Prototype Before Development?**

Without prototype:

Team builds:

❌ Wrong workflow\
❌ Missing screens\
❌ Poor usability

With prototype:

Users say:

\"This button should be here.\"

before coding starts.

**11. Prototype Validation Process**

BA conducts:

**Step 1**

Show prototype.

**Step 2**

Ask user to complete tasks.

Example:

\"Review this case.\"

**Step 3**

Observe:

-   Confusion

-   Missing information

-   User feedback

**Step 4**

Improve design.

**12. Wireframe Tools Used by BAs**

Common tools:

-   Figma

-   Balsamiq

-   Axure RP

-   Adobe XD

**13. BA Role vs UX Designer Role**

Important distinction.

A BA is not replacing a UX designer.

**BA Focus:**

-   Business needs

-   User requirements

-   Workflow

-   Functional behavior

**UX Designer Focus:**

-   Visual design

-   Interaction patterns

-   Usability

-   Design system

They collaborate.

**14. Healthcare AI Example**

**Requirement:**

\"Reviewer should validate AI recommendations.\"

BA identifies:

Need:

A review screen.

Wireframe includes:

**Left Panel:**

Original document

**Center:**

AI extracted information

**Right Panel:**

AI recommendation

**Bottom:**

Approve / Modify / Reject

Why?

Because reviewer needs comparison and control.

**15. Interview Questions**

**Q1: Why do BAs create wireframes?**

Answer:

\"Wireframes help visualize requirements, validate user expectations,
identify missing functionality, and reduce misunderstanding between
business and development teams.\"

**Q2: Difference between wireframe and prototype?**

Answer:

\"A wireframe shows structure and layout, while a prototype demonstrates
interaction and user flow.\"

**Q3: What is fidelity?**

Answer:

\"Fidelity represents the level of detail and realism in a design,
ranging from low-fidelity sketches to high-fidelity interactive
designs.\"

Create a wireframe specification for:

**AI Pharmacovigilance Copilot**

Design these screens:

**1. Login Screen**

Include:

-   User authentication

-   Role selection

**2. Dashboard**

Include:

-   Case count

-   Pending reviews

-   Risk alerts

**3. Case Review Screen**

Include:

-   Patient details

-   AI extraction

-   AI summary

-   Approval actions

**4. Approval Screen**

Include:

-   Reviewer comments

-   Approve/reject actions

**Lesson Summary**

You learned:

✅ Wireframing concepts\
✅ Prototype concepts\
✅ Low/Mid/High fidelity\
✅ Requirement to screen conversion\
✅ User flow design\
✅ Prototype validation\
✅ BA vs UX designer role\
✅ Healthcare AI example

**Lesson 21: Solution Design & Requirements Validation**

**From Requirements to an Approved Solution**

**A BA\'s responsibility does not end after writing requirements. A BA
ensures that the proposed solution actually solves the business
problem.**

Many projects fail because:

-   Requirements were misunderstood

-   Stakeholders were not aligned

-   Solution does not solve the real problem

-   Business rules were missed

-   Users reject the final product

Solution validation prevents this.

**Learning Objectives**

By the end of this lesson, you will understand:

-   What is solution design

-   BA role in solution design

-   Functional solution design

-   Non-functional considerations

-   Business rules validation

-   Requirement walkthrough

-   Design review

-   Stakeholder approval

-   Prototype feedback loop

-   Healthcare AI solution example

**1. What is Solution Design?**

Definition:

Solution design is the process of defining how a proposed solution will
meet business needs, user requirements, and technical constraints.

Simple:

Requirement:

\"Reduce pharmacovigilance processing time.\"

Solution design:

\"Create an AI-assisted workflow that extracts case data, generates
summaries, and supports human validation.\"

**2. Requirement vs Solution Design**

Very important difference.

**Requirement**

Describes:

**What the business needs**

Example:

\"Reviewer should identify serious adverse events.\"

**Solution Design**

Describes:

**How the solution will enable it**

Example:

\"AI model analyzes event description and highlights potential
seriousness indicators.\"

Comparison:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Requirement**              **Solution**
  ---------------------------- ------------------------------------------
  What                         How

  Business need                Implementation approach

  User expectation             System behavior
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**3. BA Role in Solution Design**

A BA works between:

Business

↓

Business Analyst

↓

Product / UX / Development Team

↓

Solution

BA responsibilities:

✅ Understand problem\
✅ Define solution options\
✅ Validate requirements\
✅ Identify gaps\
✅ Ensure user needs are addressed\
✅ Support design decisions

**4. Solution Design Components**

A BA considers:

**1. Functional Design**

What the system should do.

Example:

AI Pharmacovigilance Platform:

Functional requirements:

-   Upload documents

-   Extract information

-   Generate summary

-   Assign risk score

-   Allow approval

**2. Non-Functional Design**

How the system should perform.

Examples:

**Performance**

\"Dashboard loads within 3 seconds.\"

**Security**

\"Only authorized reviewers can access cases.\"

**Reliability**

\"System availability should be 99.9%.\"

**Compliance**

\"Maintain audit trail.\"

**5. Business Rules Validation**

Business rules define constraints.

Example:

Rule:

Serious adverse events must be submitted within 15 days.

BA converts this into system behavior:

IF:

Case seriousness = Serious

AND

Approval completed

THEN:

Submission deadline = 15 days

**Business Rule Format**

A BA often documents:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Field**              **Example**
  ---------------------- ------------------------------------------------
  Rule ID                BR-001

  Rule Name              Serious Case Submission

  Condition              Serious event identified

  Action                 Submit within 15 days

  Exception              Missing information
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**6. Requirement Validation**

Before development starts, BA validates:

**Is requirement complete?**

Example:

\"System should generate reports.\"

Questions:

-   Which reports?

-   Who generates?

-   Format?

-   Frequency?

-   Data source?

**Is requirement clear?**

Bad:

\"System should be fast.\"

Good:

\"System response time should be below 3 seconds.\"

**Is requirement testable?**

Bad:

\"Easy to use.\"

Good:

\"User can complete case review within 5 steps.\"

**7. Requirement Walkthrough**

Definition:

A requirement walkthrough is a structured review session where
stakeholders validate requirements before implementation.

Participants:

-   Business stakeholders

-   Product Owner

-   BA

-   Developers

-   QA

-   Compliance team

Agenda:

1.  Review requirement

2.  Explain workflow

3.  Confirm assumptions

4.  Identify missing details

5.  Approve

**Example Walkthrough**

Requirement:

\"AI should generate case summaries.\"

Questions:

Developer:

\"What input format?\"

QA:

\"How do we test accuracy?\"

Compliance:

\"Can AI output be used directly?\"

User:

\"Can I edit the summary?\"

BA captures answers.

**8. Solution Review Process**

A BA performs:

**Step 1: Review Current Problem**

Example:

Manual case processing takes 5 days.

**Step 2: Review Proposed Solution**

AI reduces manual effort.

**Step 3: Validate Against Requirements**

Check:

Does solution solve original problem?

**Step 4: Identify Gaps**

Example:

Missing:

Audit trail.

**Step 5: Get Approval**

Stakeholders accept solution.

**9. Prototype Feedback Loop**

Prototype is not final.

It is improved through feedback.

Process:

Create Prototype

↓

User Testing

↓

Collect Feedback

↓

Modify Design

↓

Validate Again

Example:

First prototype:

AI summary below document.

User feedback:

\"I need AI confidence score.\"

Update:

Add confidence indicator.

**10. Healthcare AI Solution Example**

**Problem:**

Safety reviewers spend too much time reading reports.

**Requirement:**

\"System should help reviewers process cases faster.\"

**Solution Design:**

**User Interface:**

Case dashboard

↓

AI summary screen

↓

Review screen

**Functional Design:**

System should:

1.  Upload documents

2.  Extract patient details

3.  Identify drug-event relationship

4.  Generate summary

5.  Allow reviewer approval

**Business Rules:**

-   Reviewer approval mandatory

-   AI cannot submit automatically

-   Serious cases require escalation

**Non-functional:**

-   Secure access

-   Audit logs

-   Response under 5 seconds

**11. Requirement Dependency Analysis**

Before development, BA identifies dependencies.

Example:

Feature:

AI Risk Prediction

Depends on:

-   Historical case data

-   ML model availability

-   Data quality

Dependency types:

**Internal**

Within project.

Example:

Dashboard depends on database.

**External**

Outside project.

Example:

Regulatory API integration.

**12. Decision Log**

BA maintains decisions.

Example:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Decision**                       **Reason**
  ---------------------------------- ------------------------------------
  Human approval required            Compliance

  AI cannot auto-submit              Regulatory risk

  Use existing login system          Reduce development effort
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**13. Traceability During Solution Design**

Requirement:

↓

Design

↓

Development

↓

Testing

↓

Release

Example:

BR-001

Reduce processing time

↓

FR-005

AI Summary Generation

↓

US-101

Reviewer generates summary

↓

TC-101

Verify summary generation

**14. Interview Questions**

**Q1: What is solution design?**

Answer:

\"Solution design defines how a proposed solution will satisfy business
requirements while considering functionality, usability, technical
constraints, and business rules.\"

**Q2: How does a BA validate requirements?**

Answer:

\"A BA validates requirements through walkthroughs, stakeholder reviews,
prototypes, acceptance criteria, and ensuring requirements are complete,
consistent, and testable.\"

**Q3: Difference between functional and non-functional requirements?**

Answer:

\"Functional requirements describe what the system does, while
non-functional requirements describe quality attributes such as
performance, security, and reliability.\"

**Practical Assignment**

For the AI Pharmacovigilance Copilot:

Prepare a mini Solution Design Document.

Include:

**1. Business Problem**

Example:\
Manual case processing delays.

**2. Proposed Solution**

AI-assisted case processing.

**3. Functional Requirements**

Minimum 5.

**4. Non-Functional Requirements**

Minimum 5.

**5. Business Rules**

Minimum 3.

**6. Dependencies**

Minimum 3.

**Lesson Summary**

You learned:

✅ Solution design concepts\
✅ BA role in solution design\
✅ Functional requirements\
✅ Non-functional requirements\
✅ Business rules\
✅ Requirement validation\
✅ Walkthroughs\
✅ Prototype feedback\
✅ Dependencies\
✅ Decision logs
