**PHASE 4 -- Advanced BA Execution & Industry Delivery**

**Lesson 22: BA Role in Agile Delivery**

**How a Business Analyst Works Inside a Real Scrum Team**

**A BA\'s work is not only before development. In Agile, a BA
continuously collaborates with the team throughout the product
lifecycle.**

Many beginners think:

\"BA writes requirements and gives them to developers.\"

That is a **traditional waterfall mindset**.

In Agile:

\"BA helps the team understand the problem, refine requirements,
validate solutions, and deliver business value continuously.\"

**Learning Objectives**

By the end of this lesson, you will understand:

-   BA role in Agile

-   BA vs Product Owner

-   BA activities before, during, and after Sprint

-   BA involvement in Scrum events

-   Requirement clarification

-   Working with Developers

-   Working with QA

-   Supporting UAT

-   Real healthcare AI example

**1. Where Does a BA Fit in Agile?**

A typical Agile team:

Product Owner

\|

\|

\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--

\| \|

Business Analyst Scrum Master

\|

\|

Developers \-\-\-- QA Engineers \-\-\-- UX Designers

The BA acts as a bridge:

Business Problem

↓

Business Analyst

↓

Agile Team

↓

Working Product

**2. BA Responsibilities in Agile**

A BA helps with:

**Requirement Discovery**

Understanding:

-   Business goals

-   User needs

-   Problems

**Backlog Management**

Helping create:

-   User Stories

-   Acceptance Criteria

-   Priorities

**Requirement Clarification**

During development:

-   Answer questions

-   Remove ambiguity

-   Explain business rules

**Validation**

Check:

\"Did we build the right thing?\"

**3. BA vs Product Owner**

Very common interview question.

Both roles work closely but are different.

**Product Owner Focus**

Question:

\"What should we build and why?\"

Responsibilities:

-   Product vision

-   Roadmap

-   Prioritization

-   Business value

-   Backlog ownership

**Business Analyst Focus**

Question:

\"How should the requirement be understood and implemented?\"

Responsibilities:

-   Requirement analysis

-   Process modeling

-   User stories

-   Acceptance criteria

-   Detailed clarification

**Comparison**

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Product Owner**                    **Business Analyst**
  ------------------------------------ ----------------------------------
  Owns product direction               Analyzes requirements

  Decides priority                     Defines details

  Customer value                       Requirement clarity

  Roadmap                              Execution support
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**Example:**

Requirement:

\"Create AI risk prediction feature.\"

Product Owner decides:

\"We need this feature because it reduces review time.\"

BA defines:

-   User story

-   Workflow

-   Business rules

-   Acceptance criteria

-   Data requirements

**4. BA Activities Before Sprint**

Before development begins:

**1. Requirement Gathering**

Example:

Interview safety reviewers.

Questions:

-   Current problems?

-   Required workflow?

-   Pain points?

**2. Process Analysis**

BA creates:

-   AS-IS process

-   TO-BE process

**3. User Stories**

Example:

As a safety reviewer,

I want AI to identify potential risks,

so that I can prioritize cases.

**4. Acceptance Criteria**

Example:

Given:

A case is uploaded

When:

AI analyzes the document

Then:

Risk score should be displayed.

**5. BA During Backlog Refinement**

Backlog refinement is where BA is heavily involved.

Activities:

**Explain User Stories**

Developer asks:

\"What exactly should happen?\"

BA explains.

**Identify Missing Information**

Example:

Story:

\"Generate AI summary.\"

Questions:

-   Summary format?

-   Length?

-   Editable?

-   Approval required?

**Split Large Stories**

Epic:

\"AI Case Management\"

Break into:

1.  Upload documents

2.  Extract information

3.  Generate summary

4.  Review output

**6. BA During Sprint Planning**

Sprint Planning:

Team decides:

\"What can we complete this sprint?\"

BA supports by:

**Clarifying Scope**

Example:

\"This story includes only AI extraction, not prediction.\"

**Explaining Acceptance Criteria**

**Highlighting Dependencies**

Example:

\"Dashboard depends on case database.\"

**7. BA During Development Sprint**

Many people think BA is inactive here.

Wrong.

BA continuously supports.

Activities:

**Requirement Questions**

Developer:

\"What happens if AI confidence is low?\"

BA:

\"Send case for manual review.\"

**Business Rule Clarification**

Developer:

\"Can rejected cases be edited?\"

BA:

\"Yes, before final submission.\"

**Requirement Updates**

If changes occur:

Update:

-   User story

-   Acceptance criteria

-   Documentation

**8. BA Working With Developers**

Good BA behavior:

Instead of:

\"Build exactly this.\"

Say:

\"The business problem is this. Here is the expected behavior.\"

Example:

Developer asks:

\"How should risk score display?\"

BA explains:

Business need:

Reviewer should identify high-risk cases quickly.

Possible solution:

-   Color indicator

-   Score percentage

-   Priority label

**9. BA Working With QA Team**

BA helps QA understand:

\"What should be tested?\"

Example User Story:

AI generates summary.

Acceptance Criteria:

Given:

Case document uploaded

When:

AI processing completes

Then:

Summary appears with confidence score.

QA creates:

Test:

Upload document → Verify summary.

**10. BA During Sprint Review**

Sprint Review:

Team demonstrates completed work.

BA checks:

-   Does feature solve user problem?

-   Does it match requirements?

-   Is stakeholder feedback captured?

Example:

Demo:

AI Summary Screen.

BA asks:

\"Can reviewer edit AI output?\"

If not:

New requirement created.

**11. BA During Retrospective**

Retrospective focuses on:

\"How can we improve?\"

BA contributes:

-   Requirement quality issues

-   Communication gaps

-   Process improvements

Example:

Problem:

Developers misunderstood business rules.

Improvement:

Add better acceptance criteria.

**12. BA Supporting UAT**

User Acceptance Testing:

Business users verify solution.

BA responsibilities:

-   Prepare UAT scenarios

-   Coordinate users

-   Capture defects

-   Confirm acceptance

Example:

Scenario:

\"Reviewer approves high-risk case.\"

Expected:

Audit record created.

**13. Real Healthcare AI Example**

**Project:**

AI Pharmacovigilance Copilot

**Before Sprint**

BA:

Creates:

-   User stories

-   Acceptance criteria

-   Workflow diagrams

**During Sprint**

Developer:

Builds AI extraction.

BA:

Clarifies:

-   Required fields

-   Validation rules

-   Exception handling

**QA Testing**

BA validates:

Does extraction support business needs?

**Sprint Review**

Safety reviewers test.

Feedback:

\"Need confidence score.\"

BA creates improvement story.

**14. BA Daily Activities Example**

A typical Agile BA day:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Time**           **Activity**
  ------------------ ----------------------------------------------------
  Morning            Scrum meeting

  10 AM              Developer clarification

  11 AM              Requirement analysis

  Afternoon          Stakeholder discussion

  Evening            Update stories/documentation
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**15. Interview Questions**

**Q1: What is the role of BA in Agile?**

Answer:

\"A BA helps the Agile team understand business needs, refine
requirements, create user stories, clarify requirements during
development, and ensure delivered features provide business value.\"

**Q2: Does BA replace Product Owner?**

Answer:

\"No. The Product Owner owns product vision and priorities, while the BA
focuses on requirement analysis and helping the team understand
implementation details.\"

**Q3: How does BA support developers?**

Answer:

\"BA provides requirement clarification, business rules, workflows,
acceptance criteria, and resolves ambiguity during development.\"

**Practical Assignment**

For AI Pharmacovigilance Copilot:

Create:

**User Story**

Feature:

AI Case Summary Generation

Include:

1.  User Story

2.  Acceptance Criteria

3.  Business Rules

4.  Edge Cases

5.  Dependencies

**Lesson Summary**

You learned:

✅ BA role in Agile\
✅ BA vs Product Owner\
✅ Sprint activities\
✅ Backlog refinement\
✅ Sprint planning support\
✅ Developer collaboration\
✅ QA collaboration\
✅ UAT support\
✅ Healthcare Agile example

**Lesson 23: Advanced User Stories & Acceptance Criteria Masterclass**

**Writing Requirements Like an Industry Business Analyst**

**A good BA does not write requirements as features. A good BA writes
requirements as user problems and expected outcomes.**

Many beginners write:

❌ \"Create AI dashboard.\"\
❌ \"Add risk prediction button.\"\
❌ \"Build report page.\"

These are system instructions.

An Agile BA writes:

✅ Who needs it?\
✅ What do they want?\
✅ Why do they need it?\
✅ How do we know it works?

**Learning Objectives**

By the end of this lesson, you will understand:

-   Epic → Feature → User Story hierarchy

-   User Story structure

-   INVEST principle

-   Writing professional user stories

-   Acceptance Criteria

-   Given-When-Then format

-   Business rules

-   Negative scenarios

-   Edge cases

-   AI product examples

**1. Requirement Hierarchy in Agile**

Large requirements are broken into smaller pieces.

Structure:

Product Vision

↓

Epic

↓

Feature

↓

User Story

↓

Task

**Example: AI Pharmacovigilance Platform**

**Product Vision**

\"Reduce adverse event processing time using AI.\"

**Epic**

AI-Assisted Case Processing

**Features**

1.  AI Document Extraction

2.  AI Summary Generation

3.  Risk Prediction

4.  Automated Classification

**User Stories**

Example:

\"As a safety reviewer, I want AI to extract patient information so that
I can reduce manual data entry.\"

**2. What is a User Story?**

Definition:

A user story is a short description of a user\'s need written from the
user\'s perspective.

Standard format:

As a \<user\>

I want \<goal\>

So that \<benefit\>

Example:

As a pharmacovigilance reviewer,

I want AI to generate case summaries,

So that I can review adverse events faster.

**3. Three Important Parts**

**1. User (Who?)**

Who needs this?

Examples:

-   Safety reviewer

-   Doctor

-   Patient

-   Administrator

**2. Goal (What?)**

What capability is needed?

Example:

Generate AI summary.

**3. Benefit (Why?)**

Why does it matter?

Example:

Reduce review time.

**4. Bad vs Good User Stories**

**Bad:**

\"Create AI module.\"

Problems:

❌ No user\
❌ No purpose\
❌ No value

**Good:**

\"As a safety reviewer, I want AI to identify potential safety risks so
that I can prioritize urgent cases.\"

**5. INVEST Principle**

A professional user story should follow INVEST.

Created by:

Bill Wake

**I -- Independent**

Story should stand alone.

Bad:

\"Create approval button.\"

Depends on:

\"Create review screen.\"

Better:

\"Reviewer can approve validated cases.\"

**N -- Negotiable**

Story is not a fixed contract.

The team can discuss implementation.

**V -- Valuable**

Must provide user/business value.

Bad:

\"Add database field.\"

No user value.

**E -- Estimable**

Team should understand enough to estimate effort.

**S -- Small**

Should fit inside a sprint.

**T -- Testable**

Should have clear acceptance criteria.

**6. User Story Example (Healthcare AI)**

**Story:**

As a safety reviewer,

I want the system to highlight serious adverse events,

So that I can prioritize high-risk cases.

Acceptance Criteria:

1.  

Given a case is uploaded,

When AI analyzes the event,

Then serious indicators should be displayed.

2.  

Given a high-risk case,

When reviewer opens dashboard,

Then the case should appear as high priority.

**7. Acceptance Criteria**

Definition:

Acceptance criteria define the conditions that must be satisfied for a
user story to be considered complete.

Simple:

User Story:

\"What user needs\"

Acceptance Criteria:

\"How we verify it works\"

Example:

Story:

AI generates summary.

Acceptance Criteria:

-   Summary generated after document upload

-   User can edit summary

-   System stores final version

-   Audit log is created

**8. Given-When-Then Format**

Most common Agile format.

Also called:

BDD (Behavior Driven Development)

Structure:

Given

(initial condition)

When

(action)

Then

(expected result)

Example:

Feature:

AI Risk Classification

Scenario:

High-risk case identification

Given a safety case contains serious adverse event indicators

When AI analyzes the case

Then the system should mark the case as high risk

**9. Multiple Acceptance Criteria Example**

**User Story:**

\"As a reviewer, I want AI-generated summaries so that I can reduce
review time.\"

**AC1: Generate Summary**

Given a document is uploaded

When AI processing completes

Then a summary should be displayed

**AC2: Confidence Score**

Given AI generates a summary

When confidence is calculated

Then confidence percentage should be visible

**AC3: Low Confidence**

Given AI confidence is below threshold

When summary is generated

Then case should be flagged for manual review

**10. Business Rules Inside User Stories**

Business rules define restrictions.

Example:

Rule:

\"Only medical reviewers can approve serious cases.\"

User Story:

\"As a medical reviewer, I want to approve serious cases so that
compliance requirements are followed.\"

Acceptance Criteria:

Given a serious case

When a safety reviewer attempts approval

Then approval option should not be available

**11. Negative Scenarios**

Professional BAs always consider failures.

Example:

Normal:

Upload document → AI processes → Summary generated

Negative:

Upload corrupted file.

Expected:

System shows:

\"Unable to process document. Upload valid file.\"

**12. Edge Cases**

Edge cases are unusual situations.

Example:

AI Summary Feature:

Normal:

100-page document.

Edge:

1000-page document.

Questions:

-   Should processing continue?

-   Should user receive notification?

-   Should system split document?

**13. Definition of Ready (DoR)**

Before development starts, story should be ready.

Checklist:

✅ Clear user value\
✅ Acceptance criteria defined\
✅ Dependencies identified\
✅ Estimated\
✅ Stakeholders agree

**14. Definition of Done (DoD)**

When is story complete?

Example:

AI Summary Story:

✅ Code completed\
✅ Tested\
✅ Security checked\
✅ User acceptance completed\
✅ Documentation updated

**15. Epic Breakdown Example**

Epic:

AI Case Processing

Feature:

AI Document Extraction

Stories:

**Story 1**

Upload document

**Story 2**

Extract patient details

**Story 3**

Extract drug information

**Story 4**

Validate extracted data

**Story 5**

Store extracted information

**16. Complete BA Example**

**Feature:**

AI Risk Prediction

**User Story:**

As a safety reviewer,

I want AI to predict case risk level,

So that I can prioritize critical cases.

**Acceptance Criteria:**

**Scenario 1:**

Given a case contains complete information

When AI analyzes the case

Then risk score should be generated

**Scenario 2:**

Given risk score is high

When reviewer opens dashboard

Then case should be highlighted as priority

**Scenario 3:**

Given AI confidence is low

When prediction is generated

Then reviewer should receive a warning

**17. Interview Questions**

**Q1: What is a user story?**

Answer:

\"A user story is a short description of a user\'s requirement written
from the user\'s perspective, explaining who needs something, what they
need, and why.\"

**Q2: What is INVEST?**

Answer:

\"INVEST defines qualities of a good user story: Independent,
Negotiable, Valuable, Estimable, Small, and Testable.\"

**Q3: Difference between requirement and acceptance criteria?**

Answer:

\"Requirement describes the capability needed, while acceptance criteria
define conditions to verify that the capability works correctly.\"

**Practical Assignment**

Create user stories for:

**AI Pharmacovigilance Copilot**

Create 5 stories:

1.  Document Upload

2.  AI Extraction

3.  AI Summary

4.  Risk Prediction

5.  Regulatory Submission

For each include:

-   User Story

-   Acceptance Criteria (Given-When-Then)

-   Business Rules

-   Edge Cases

**Lesson Summary**

You learned:

✅ Epic → Feature → Story hierarchy\
✅ User Story writing\
✅ INVEST principle\
✅ Acceptance Criteria\
✅ Given-When-Then\
✅ Business rules\
✅ Negative scenarios\
✅ Edge cases\
✅ Definition of Ready\
✅ Definition of Done

**Lesson 24: Advanced Requirements Documentation**

**BRD, FRD, SRS, RTM & Professional BA Documentation**

**A Business Analyst is judged not only by understanding requirements
but also by the quality of documentation created to communicate those
requirements.**

In real projects, a BA creates different documents for different
audiences.

Example:

Business executives need:

\"Why are we building this?\"

Developers need:

\"What exactly should the system do?\"

QA needs:

\"How do we verify it works?\"

Each requires different documentation.

**Learning Objectives**

By the end of this lesson, you will understand:

-   BA documentation ecosystem

-   BRD vs FRD vs SRS

-   Business Requirements Document

-   Functional Requirements Document

-   Software Requirements Specification

-   Requirement Traceability Matrix

-   Business Rules Document

-   When to use each document

-   Healthcare AI examples

**1. BA Documentation Landscape**

A typical BA documentation flow:

Business Need

↓

BRD

(Business Requirements)

↓

FRD

(Functional Requirements)

↓

SRS

(System Requirements)

↓

User Stories

↓

Development

↓

Testing

**2. BRD (Business Requirements Document)**

**What is BRD?**

Definition:

BRD describes the business problem, objectives, goals, scope, and
high-level requirements from a business perspective.

Audience:

-   Business leaders

-   Sponsors

-   Product owners

-   Stakeholders

**BRD Answers:**

**Why are we building this?**

**What business problem are we solving?**

**What outcome do we expect?**

**BRD Structure**

A professional BRD contains:

**1. Document Information**

Example:

Project Name:

AI Pharmacovigilance Copilot

Version:

1.0

Author:

Business Analyst

**2. Executive Summary**

Example:

\"The organization wants to reduce adverse event processing time using
AI-assisted automation.\"

**3. Business Problem**

Current situation:

-   Manual review process

-   Long processing time

-   High workload

**4. Business Objectives**

Example:

Reduce case processing time by 50%.

**5. Scope**

**In Scope:**

✅ AI document extraction\
✅ Case summarization\
✅ Risk identification

**Out of Scope:**

❌ Fully automated regulatory submission approval

**6. Stakeholders**

Example:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Stakeholder**                 **Role**
  ------------------------------- ---------------------------------------
  Safety Team                     Primary users

  Medical Team                    Approval

  Compliance                      Regulatory review
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**7. Business Requirements**

Example:

BR-001:

\"The system should reduce manual case review effort.\"

**8. Success Metrics**

Example:

Before:

5 days processing

After:

2 days processing

**3. FRD (Functional Requirements Document)**

**What is FRD?**

Definition:

FRD describes what the system must do to satisfy business requirements.

Audience:

-   Developers

-   QA engineers

-   Architects

BRD says:

\"We need faster case processing.\"

FRD says:

\"System shall extract patient information automatically from uploaded
documents.\"

**FRD Structure**

**1. Functional Requirement ID**

Example:

FR-001

**2. Feature Name**

AI Document Extraction

**3. Description**

System extracts:

-   Patient information

-   Drug details

-   Event information

**4. User Interaction**

Example:

1.  User uploads document

2.  System processes file

3.  Extracted information displayed

**5. Business Rules**

Example:

Only authorized reviewers can edit extracted data.

**6. Acceptance Criteria**

Example:

Given:

Document uploaded

When:

AI processing completes

Then:

Extracted fields should display.

**4. SRS (Software Requirements Specification)**

**What is SRS?**

Definition:

SRS describes complete software requirements including functional and
non-functional requirements.

It is more technical than BRD/FRD.

Audience:

-   Developers

-   Architects

-   QA

-   Technical teams

SRS Includes:

**Functional Requirements**

What system does.

Example:

Login functionality.

**Non-Functional Requirements**

How system performs.

Example:

Response time \< 3 seconds.

**External Interfaces**

Example:

Integration with:

-   Regulatory database

-   Drug database

**Security Requirements**

Example:

Role-based access control.

**Data Requirements**

Example:

Store:

-   Case ID

-   Patient information

-   Drug details

**5. BRD vs FRD vs SRS**

Very important interview question.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **BRD**         **FRD**               **SRS**
  --------------- --------------------- ---------------------------------
  Business need   System functionality  Complete software specification

  Why             What                  How system behaves

  Business users  BA + Dev              Technical team

  High level      Detailed              Detailed + technical
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

Example:

**BRD:**

\"Reduce manual pharmacovigilance workload.\"

**FRD:**

\"System shall generate AI summaries.\"

**SRS:**

\"AI service shall process PDF documents using NLP pipeline and return
summary within 10 seconds.\"

**6. Requirement Traceability Matrix (RTM)**

**What is RTM?**

Definition:

RTM ensures every requirement is tracked from business need to testing.

Flow:

Business Requirement

↓

Functional Requirement

↓

User Story

↓

Development Task

↓

Test Case

**RTM Example**

  -------------------------------------------------------------------------
  **BR ID**    **FR ID**    **User Story**                 **Test Case**
  ------------ ------------ ------------------------------ ----------------
  BR-001       FR-001       Upload Document                TC-001

  BR-002       FR-002       AI Summary                     TC-002
  -------------------------------------------------------------------------

**Why RTM is Important?**

✅ Prevent missing requirements\
✅ Supports audits\
✅ Helps impact analysis\
✅ Ensures complete testing

**7. Business Rules Document**

Business rules define policies and decisions.

Example:

Rule:

\"Serious adverse events must be reviewed by medical experts.\"

Format:

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  **ID**      **Rule**             **Condition**   **Action**
  ----------- -------------------- --------------- --------------------------
  BRULE-001   Serious Case Review  Risk = High     Medical approval required

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

**8. Acceptance Criteria Document**

Sometimes maintained separately.

Contains:

-   User scenarios

-   Expected behavior

-   Validation rules

Example:

Feature:

AI Risk Score

Criteria:

Given case information exists

When AI analyzes case

Then risk score must be displayed

**9. Change Request Document**

Used when requirements change.

Example:

New request:

\"Add multilingual AI support.\"

Document contains:

-   Change description

-   Reason

-   Impact

-   Approval

**10. Complete Enterprise Example**

**Project:**

AI Pharmacovigilance Platform

**BRD**

Business Goal:

Reduce case processing time.

**FRD**

Requirement:

System should extract adverse event information.

**SRS**

Technical requirement:

NLP engine processes uploaded documents and returns structured fields.

**User Story**

\"As a reviewer, I want AI extraction so that I can reduce manual
entry.\"

**Acceptance Criteria**

Given:

PDF uploaded

When:

Extraction completes

Then:

Patient and drug fields are populated.

**Test Case**

Upload sample report.

Expected:

Fields extracted correctly.

**11. BA Documentation Best Practices**

**1. Use unique IDs**

Example:

BR-001

FR-001

US-001

**2. Avoid ambiguous words**

Avoid:

\"Fast\"

Use:

\"Response time below 3 seconds.\"

**3. Keep traceability**

Every requirement should connect to testing.

**4. Version control**

Maintain:

-   Version number

-   Date

-   Author

-   Change history

**5. Review before approval**

Never send incomplete documents.

**12. Interview Questions**

**Q1: Difference between BRD and FRD?**

Answer:

\"BRD defines business objectives and needs, while FRD defines the
system functionalities required to achieve those objectives.\"

**Q2: What is RTM?**

Answer:

\"RTM is a document that maps requirements throughout the lifecycle from
business requirement to implementation and testing.\"

**Q3: Why are acceptance criteria important?**

Answer:

\"Acceptance criteria remove ambiguity and provide clear conditions to
determine whether a requirement is successfully implemented.\"

**Practical Assignment**

Create documentation structure for:

**AI Pharmacovigilance Copilot**

Prepare:

**1. BRD**

Include:

-   Business problem

-   Objectives

-   Scope

-   Stakeholders

**2. FRD**

Include:

-   5 functional requirements

**3. RTM**

Map:

BR → FR → User Story → Test Case

**Lesson Summary**

You learned:

✅ BRD\
✅ FRD\
✅ SRS\
✅ RTM\
✅ Business Rules Document\
✅ Acceptance Criteria Document\
✅ Change Request Document\
✅ Documentation best practices\
✅ Enterprise healthcare example

**Lesson 25: Stakeholder Management & Communication Masterclass**

**Managing People, Expectations, Conflicts, and Decisions**

**A BA spends less time writing documents and more time creating
alignment between people.**

Many projects fail not because of technology problems, but because:

-   Stakeholders have different expectations

-   Requirements are misunderstood

-   Decisions are delayed

-   Teams are not aligned

A senior BA is an **alignment specialist**.

**Learning Objectives**

By the end of this lesson, you will understand:

-   Who stakeholders are

-   Stakeholder identification techniques

-   Stakeholder analysis

-   Power-Interest Matrix

-   Stakeholder communication strategy

-   Managing conflicts

-   Requirement negotiation

-   Handling difficult stakeholders

-   Healthcare AI example

**1. What is Stakeholder Management?**

Definition:

Stakeholder management is the process of identifying, analyzing,
communicating with, and managing people who influence or are affected by
a project.

Simple:

A stakeholder is anyone who:

-   Wants something from the project

-   Can impact the project

-   Is affected by the outcome

**2. Who is a Stakeholder?**

Stakeholders can be:

**Internal**

Inside organization:

-   Product Owner

-   Business team

-   Developers

-   QA

-   Management

-   Compliance

**External**

Outside organization:

-   Customers

-   Regulators

-   Vendors

-   Partners

**Healthcare AI Example**

Project:

AI Pharmacovigilance Platform

Stakeholders:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Stakeholder**                 **Interest**
  ------------------------------- ---------------------------------------
  Safety Reviewer                 Faster case review

  Medical Reviewer                Clinical accuracy

  Compliance Team                 Regulatory compliance

  IT Team                         Security

  Management                      ROI

  Patients                        Safety improvement
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**3. Stakeholder Identification**

A BA identifies stakeholders early.

Methods:

**1. Organizational Analysis**

Look at:

-   Departments

-   Roles

-   Decision makers

**2. Process Analysis**

Ask:

\"Who performs each step?\"

Example:

Case approval process:

-   Reviewer

-   Medical expert

-   Compliance officer

**3. User Research**

Understand:

-   Who uses product?

-   Who benefits?

-   Who may resist?

**4. Stakeholder Register**

A BA maintains stakeholder information.

Example:

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Stakeholder**       **Role**         **Influence**     **Interest**
  --------------------- ---------------- ----------------- ---------------
  Safety Head           Sponsor          High              High

  Reviewer              User             Medium            High

  IT Manager            Technical        High              Medium
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------

**5. Stakeholder Analysis**

Purpose:

Understand:

-   Who matters most?

-   Who needs more communication?

-   Who can block progress?

Important factors:

**Power**

Ability to influence decisions.

Example:

CEO has high power.

**Interest**

How much they care.

Example:

Daily user has high interest.

**6. Power-Interest Matrix**

One of the most important BA techniques.

Four categories:

HIGH POWER

Manage Closely

\|

\|

Keep Satisfied \| Key Players

\|

LOW INTEREST \-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-- HIGH INTEREST

\|

Monitor \| Keep Informed

\|

LOW POWER

**Category 1: Manage Closely**

High Power + High Interest

Examples:

-   Product Owner

-   Project Sponsor

Strategy:

Frequent communication.

**Category 2: Keep Satisfied**

High Power + Low Interest

Example:

Senior executives.

Strategy:

Provide important updates.

Do not overload.

**Category 3: Keep Informed**

Low Power + High Interest

Example:

End users.

Strategy:

Regular communication.

**Category 4: Monitor**

Low Power + Low Interest

Example:

Indirect teams.

Strategy:

Minimal updates.

**7. Stakeholder Communication Plan**

A BA defines:

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Stakeholder**       **Information**     **Method**     **Frequency**
  --------------------- ------------------- -------------- ---------------
  Product Owner         Progress            Meeting        Weekly

  Developers            Requirements        Workshop       Daily

  Users                 Feedback            Demo           Sprint end
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------

**8. Communication Styles**

Different stakeholders need different communication.

**Executives**

Need:

-   Business value

-   Cost

-   ROI

-   Risk

Avoid:

Technical details.

**Developers**

Need:

-   Logic

-   Rules

-   Edge cases

-   Data behavior

**Users**

Need:

-   Workflow

-   Benefits

-   Usability

**9. Requirement Negotiation**

Real projects have conflicts.

Example:

Business says:

\"We want AI to automatically approve cases.\"

Compliance says:

\"Human approval is mandatory.\"

BA role:

Find balance.

Negotiation steps:

**Step 1:**

Understand the conflict.

**Step 2:**

Identify underlying need.

**Step 3:**

Find alternatives.

**Step 4:**

Document decision.

**Example Resolution**

Original request:

AI automatically approves cases.

Alternative:

AI recommends approval.

Human reviewer confirms.

Result:

Business gets speed.

Compliance gets control.

**10. Managing Difficult Stakeholders**

Common situations:

**Situation 1:**

Stakeholder keeps changing requirements.

Problem:

Scope creep.

BA approach:

Ask:

\"Has the business objective changed?\"

Evaluate:

-   Impact

-   Priority

-   Cost

**Situation 2:**

Stakeholder does not provide information.

BA approach:

-   Schedule focused workshop

-   Provide questions beforehand

-   Explain impact of delay

**Situation 3:**

Two stakeholders disagree.

BA approach:

Focus on:

-   Business objective

-   Data

-   User impact

Not personal opinions.

**11. Conflict Resolution Techniques**

Common techniques:

**Collaborating**

Best approach.

Find win-win solution.

Example:

AI assistance + human approval.

**Compromising**

Both sides give something.

**Avoiding**

Use only when issue is minor.

**Accommodating**

One side accepts.

**Competing**

Used for urgent decisions.

**12. Stakeholder Workshop Facilitation**

A BA often conducts workshops.

Workshop structure:

**Before Workshop**

Prepare:

-   Agenda

-   Participants

-   Questions

-   Documents

**During Workshop**

BA:

-   Controls discussion

-   Captures decisions

-   Clarifies ambiguity

**After Workshop**

Share:

-   Meeting notes

-   Decisions

-   Action items

**13. Decision Management**

BA maintains decision log.

Example:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Decision**                  **Owner**     **Reason**
  ----------------------------- ------------- ---------------------------
  Human approval required       Compliance    Regulatory requirement

  AI confidence displayed       Product       Improve trust
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**14. Healthcare AI Stakeholder Example**

**Project:**

AI Pharmacovigilance Copilot

Stakeholder Conflict:

**Business Team:**

\"We need maximum automation.\"

**Compliance:**

\"AI cannot make final decisions.\"

**Users:**

\"We need faster workflow.\"

BA solution:

Design:

AI recommends

↓

Reviewer validates

↓

System records audit trail

Everyone\'s goal achieved.

**15. Interview Questions**

**Q1: How do you identify stakeholders?**

Answer:

\"I identify stakeholders by analyzing business processes,
organizational structure, user roles, decision makers, and anyone
impacted by the solution.\"

**Q2: How do you handle conflicting requirements?**

Answer:

\"I understand the underlying business objectives, analyze impact,
facilitate discussion, evaluate alternatives, and document the final
decision.\"

**Q3: What is a stakeholder matrix?**

Answer:

\"A stakeholder matrix categorizes stakeholders based on factors like
power and interest to determine the appropriate engagement strategy.\"

**Practical Assignment**

For AI Pharmacovigilance Copilot:

Create:

**1. Stakeholder Register**

Include 8 stakeholders.

Columns:

-   Name/Role

-   Interest

-   Influence

-   Communication Method

**2. Power-Interest Matrix**

Classify:

-   High Power + High Interest

-   High Power + Low Interest

-   Low Power + High Interest

-   Low Power + Low Interest

**3. Conflict Scenario**

Create one conflict and explain BA resolution.

**Lesson Summary**

You learned:

✅ Stakeholder identification\
✅ Stakeholder register\
✅ Stakeholder analysis\
✅ Power-interest matrix\
✅ Communication planning\
✅ Requirement negotiation\
✅ Conflict resolution\
✅ Workshop facilitation\
✅ Decision management\
✅ Healthcare AI example

**Lesson 26: Requirements Elicitation Masterclass (Advanced)**

**Discovering the Right Requirements from Stakeholders**

**A BA does not collect requirements. A BA discovers the real business
need behind requirements.**

A stakeholder may say:

\"We need an AI dashboard.\"

A beginner BA writes:

\"Create dashboard.\"

A senior BA asks:

-   Why do you need it?

-   What problem are you solving?

-   Who will use it?

-   What decisions will it support?

-   What happens today without it?

The difference between an average BA and a senior BA is the **quality of
questions asked.**

**Learning Objectives**

By the end of this lesson, you will understand:

-   What is requirement elicitation

-   Requirement elicitation vs requirement gathering

-   Elicitation techniques

-   Interview techniques

-   Workshop facilitation

-   Observation technique

-   Document analysis

-   Brainstorming

-   Prototyping as elicitation

-   Powerful BA questioning techniques

-   Healthcare AI example

**1. What is Requirement Elicitation?**

Definition:

Requirement elicitation is the process of discovering, understanding,
and documenting stakeholder needs, expectations, problems, and business
objectives.

Simple:

Finding:

\"What does the business actually need?\"

**2. Requirement Gathering vs Requirement Elicitation**

Important interview difference.

**Requirement Gathering**

Means:

Collecting information provided by stakeholders.

Example:

Stakeholder says:

\"We need faster reports.\"

BA writes it.

**Requirement Elicitation**

Means:

Investigating the real need.

BA asks:

Why are reports slow?

Answer:

Because analysts manually combine data from five systems.

Actual need:

Automated data integration.

Comparison:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Gathering**                            **Elicitation**
  ---------------------------------------- ------------------------------
  Collect information                      Discover needs

  Passive                                  Active

  Stakeholder-driven                       BA-driven

  Surface requirement                      Root problem
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**3. Elicitation Process**

A professional BA follows:

Prepare

↓

Identify Stakeholders

↓

Conduct Elicitation

↓

Analyze Information

↓

Validate Requirements

↓

Document

**4. Elicitation Techniques**

BABOK defines many techniques.

Most important for BAs:

1.  Interviews

2.  Workshops

3.  Observation

4.  Brainstorming

5.  Document Analysis

6.  Surveys

7.  Prototyping

8.  Focus Groups

**5. Technique 1: Interviews**

The most common technique.

Definition:

A structured conversation between BA and stakeholder to discover
requirements.

Types:

**Structured Interview**

Fixed questions.

Example:

\"What fields are required?\"

**Unstructured Interview**

Open discussion.

Example:

\"Tell me about your current process.\"

**Semi-Structured**

Combination.

Most commonly used by BAs.

**6. Effective BA Interview Questions**

Weak question:

\"Do you need AI?\"

Better:

\"What challenges do you face while reviewing cases?\"

Powerful questions:

**Current Process**

\"How do you perform this activity today?\"

**Problem Discovery**

\"What is the biggest difficulty in this process?\"

**Impact**

\"What happens if this problem continues?\"

**Frequency**

\"How often does this occur?\"

**Exceptions**

\"What happens when something goes wrong?\"

**Future State**

\"What would an ideal process look like?\"

**7. Healthcare AI Interview Example**

Stakeholder:

Safety Reviewer

Bad BA:

\"Do you want AI summary?\"

Answer:

\"Yes.\"

Requirement:

AI summary.

Good BA:

Questions:

**Current Process**

\"How do you review adverse event reports today?\"

Answer:

\"I read PDF reports manually.\"

**Problem**

\"What takes most time?\"

Answer:

\"Finding relevant medical information.\"

**Improvement**

\"What would help?\"

Answer:

\"A summary highlighting important information.\"

Actual requirement:

AI-assisted information extraction and summarization.

**8. Technique 2: Workshops**

Definition:

A workshop is a collaborative session where multiple stakeholders
discuss, analyze, and define requirements.

Used when:

-   Many stakeholders involved

-   Requirements are complex

-   Decisions needed

Participants:

Example:

AI Pharmacovigilance:

-   Safety reviewers

-   Medical experts

-   Compliance

-   IT team

-   Product owner

**Workshop Agenda Example**

**Topic:**

Design AI Case Review Workflow

**Step 1**

Explain current process

**Step 2**

Identify pain points

**Step 3**

Design future workflow

**Step 4**

Define requirements

**Step 5**

Confirm decisions

**9. BA Workshop Skills**

A good facilitator:

✅ Controls discussion\
✅ Encourages participation\
✅ Avoids domination by one person\
✅ Captures decisions\
✅ Manages conflicts

**10. Technique 3: Observation**

Definition:

Observing users while they perform their actual work.

Why useful?

People often cannot explain their process accurately.

Example:

Reviewer says:

\"I review cases quickly.\"

Observation shows:

-   Opens multiple systems

-   Copies information manually

-   Searches databases

BA discovers:

Integration requirement.

**11. Technique 4: Document Analysis**

BA studies existing documents.

Examples:

-   SOPs

-   Policies

-   Reports

-   Existing systems

-   Regulations

Healthcare example:

Study:

Pharmacovigilance SOP

Discover:

-   Approval rules

-   Mandatory fields

-   Compliance requirements

**12. Technique 5: Brainstorming**

Definition:

A group technique to generate ideas and possible solutions.

Rules:

During idea generation:

❌ No criticism

✅ Encourage ideas

Example:

Question:

\"How can we reduce case review time?\"

Ideas:

-   AI summary

-   Automation

-   Better dashboard

-   Integration

**13. Technique 6: Prototyping as Elicitation**

Very powerful.

Sometimes users cannot describe what they want.

Show them something.

Example:

Ask:

\"What should dashboard contain?\"

User:

\"I don\'t know.\"

Show prototype.

User:

\"I need risk score here.\"

Prototype reveals hidden requirements.

**14. Asking the 5 Whys Technique**

Used for root cause discovery.

Example:

Problem:

Case processing is delayed.

Why?

Because reviewers spend too much time reading documents.

Why?

Because information is scattered.

Why?

Because systems are not integrated.

Root cause:

Lack of integrated information access.

Solution:

AI-assisted unified case view.

**15. Requirements Elicitation Challenges**

**Challenge 1:**

Stakeholder says:

\"Build what I asked.\"

BA response:

Understand objective first.

**Challenge 2:**

Stakeholders disagree.

BA:

Facilitate discussion.

**Challenge 3:**

Users cannot explain needs.

BA:

Use:

-   Prototype

-   Observation

-   Examples

**16. Elicitation Output**

After elicitation, BA produces:

-   Business requirements

-   User stories

-   Process models

-   Business rules

-   Acceptance criteria

-   Use cases

**17. Complete Healthcare AI Example**

**Goal:**

Build AI Pharmacovigilance Platform

**Elicitation Activities:**

**Interview:**

Safety reviewers

Discover:

Manual document review problem.

**Workshop:**

Design future workflow.

**Observation:**

Understand daily tasks.

**Document Analysis:**

Review SOP requirements.

**Prototype:**

Validate AI dashboard.

Output:

Requirements ready for development.

**18. Interview Questions**

**Q1: What is requirement elicitation?**

Answer:

\"Requirement elicitation is the process of discovering stakeholder
needs, business objectives, problems, and expectations using various
analysis techniques.\"

**Q2: Difference between gathering and elicitation?**

Answer:

\"Gathering collects stated requirements, while elicitation explores the
underlying business needs and problems.\"

**Q3: Which elicitation technique do you prefer?**

Answer:

\"The technique depends on the situation. For complex products I prefer
combining interviews, workshops, observation, and prototyping.\"

**Practical Assignment**

For AI Pharmacovigilance Copilot:

Perform a mini elicitation exercise.

Create:

**Stakeholder Interview**

Stakeholder:

Safety Reviewer

Prepare:

1.  10 interview questions

**Current Process**

Document:

AS-IS workflow

**Pain Points**

Identify:

Minimum 5 problems

**Future Requirements**

Create:

Minimum 5 requirements

**Lesson Summary**

You learned:

✅ Requirement elicitation\
✅ Gathering vs elicitation\
✅ Interviews\
✅ Workshops\
✅ Observation\
✅ Document analysis\
✅ Brainstorming\
✅ Prototyping\
✅ 5 Whys technique\
✅ Healthcare AI elicitation example

**Lesson 27: Business Analysis Techniques Masterclass**

**The Core BA Toolkit for Problem Solving & Decision Making**

**A BA is not only a requirement writer. A BA is a problem solver who
analyzes situations, identifies gaps, finds root causes, evaluates
options, and recommends solutions.**

In real projects, stakeholders rarely come with perfect requirements.

They come with problems:

-   \"Our process is too slow.\"

-   \"Users are unhappy.\"

-   \"The system has too many errors.\"

-   \"We need automation.\"

The BA applies analysis techniques to convert problems into solutions.

**Learning Objectives**

By the end of this lesson, you will understand:

-   SWOT Analysis

-   Gap Analysis

-   Root Cause Analysis (RCA)

-   Impact Analysis

-   Decision Matrix

-   MoSCoW Prioritization

-   Feasibility Analysis

-   Risk Analysis

-   Healthcare AI examples

**1. SWOT Analysis**

**What is SWOT?**

SWOT stands for:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Letter**             **Meaning**
  ---------------------- ------------------------------------------------
  S                      Strengths

  W                      Weaknesses

  O                      Opportunities

  T                      Threats
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

Definition:

SWOT analysis evaluates internal strengths and weaknesses and external
opportunities and threats related to a business initiative.

**Example: AI Pharmacovigilance Platform**

**Strengths**

Internal advantages:

✅ AI reduces manual work\
✅ Faster case processing\
✅ Better data organization

**Weaknesses**

Internal limitations:

❌ AI accuracy depends on data quality\
❌ Initial implementation cost

**Opportunities**

External possibilities:

✅ Regulatory AI adoption\
✅ Healthcare automation growth\
✅ Global expansion

**Threats**

External risks:

❌ Regulatory restrictions\
❌ Data privacy concerns\
❌ Competitor solutions

**SWOT Matrix**

INTERNAL

Strengths Weaknesses

AI automation Data dependency

Fast processing Implementation cost

EXTERNAL

Opportunities Threats

Market growth Regulations

AI adoption Privacy risks

**When BA Uses SWOT?**

Use during:

-   New product analysis

-   Business case creation

-   Strategy discussions

-   Solution evaluation

**2. Gap Analysis**

One of the most important BA techniques.

**Definition:**

Gap Analysis identifies the difference between the current state (AS-IS)
and desired future state (TO-BE).

Simple:

Where are we now?

↓

Where do we want to be?

↓

What is missing?

**Example:**

**Current State (AS-IS)**

Pharmacovigilance process:

-   Manual document review

-   5 days processing

-   Multiple systems

**Future State (TO-BE)**

AI-assisted process:

-   Automated extraction

-   2 days processing

-   Unified dashboard

**Gap**

  --------------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Area**        **Current**   **Future**      **Gap**
  --------------- ------------- --------------- ----------------------------
  Data Entry      Manual        Automated       AI extraction needed

  Review          Slow          Faster          AI summary

  Reporting       Manual        Automated       Integration
  --------------------------------------------------------------------------

**BA Output:**

Gap Register

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Gap ID**  **Gap**                 **Impact**     **Solution**
  ----------- ----------------------- -------------- ---------------------
  GAP-001     Manual extraction       High effort    NLP automation

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------

**3. Root Cause Analysis (RCA)**

**Problem:**

A symptom is not always the real problem.

Definition:

Root Cause Analysis identifies the underlying reason behind a problem.

Example:

Problem:

\"Case processing is delayed.\"

Wrong conclusion:

Need faster employees.

RCA:

Why delayed?

↓

Reviewers spend time searching information.

Why?

↓

Data exists in multiple systems.

Why?

↓

No integration.

Root Cause:

Disconnected systems.

**RCA Techniques**

Common methods:

1.  5 Whys

2.  Fishbone Diagram

3.  Fault Tree Analysis

**5 Whys Example**

Problem:

AI project adoption is low.

Why 1:

Users don\'t trust AI output.

Why 2:

AI explanations are unclear.

Why 3:

No confidence score provided.

Root Cause:

Lack of explainability.

Solution:

Add AI transparency features.

**4. Fishbone Diagram (Ishikawa)**

Used to categorize possible causes.

Categories:

**People**

Training issues

**Process**

Poor workflow

**Technology**

System limitations

**Data**

Poor quality

**Environment**

External factors

Example:

Problem:

High case review errors.

Possible causes:

People:

Insufficient training

Process:

Unclear SOP

Technology:

Poor validation

Data:

Incomplete records

**5. Impact Analysis**

**Definition:**

Impact Analysis evaluates how a change affects systems, processes,
users, cost, and risks.

Whenever someone requests a change:

BA asks:

\"What will this affect?\"

Example Change:

Add multilingual AI support.

Impact:

**User Impact**

Reviewers can work in multiple languages.

**Process Impact**

Translation workflow required.

**Technical Impact**

New AI language models.

**Cost Impact**

Additional infrastructure.

**Risk Impact**

Translation accuracy.

**Impact Analysis Template**

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Area**               **Impact**
  ---------------------- ------------------------------------------------
  Process                Workflow change

  System                 New capability

  User                   Training required

  Data                   New fields

  Risk                   Quality concerns
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**6. Decision Matrix**

Used when choosing between alternatives.

Definition:

A decision matrix compares options using evaluation criteria and
weighted scores.

Example:

Choosing AI Platform.

Options:

A. Build internally

B. Buy external solution

C. Hybrid

Criteria:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Criteria**                               **Weight**
  ------------------------------------------ ----------------------------
  Cost                                       30%

  Accuracy                                   40%

  Integration                                30%
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

Scoring:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Option**                              **Score**
  --------------------------------------- -------------------------------
  Build                                   7.5

  Buy                                     8

  Hybrid                                  9
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

Decision:

Hybrid approach selected.

**7. MoSCoW Prioritization**

Used for requirement prioritization.

MoSCoW:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Letter**               **Meaning**
  ------------------------ ----------------------------------------------
  M                        Must Have

  S                        Should Have

  C                        Could Have

  W                        Won\'t Have
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

Example:

AI Pharmacovigilance MVP

**Must Have**

✅ Upload case\
✅ AI extraction\
✅ Reviewer approval

**Should Have**

✅ Risk prediction

**Could Have**

✅ Voice assistant

**Won\'t Have**

❌ Fully automatic approval

**8. Feasibility Analysis**

Before starting a project:

\"Can we realistically build this?\"

Types:

**Technical Feasibility**

Can technology support it?

Example:

Is AI accuracy acceptable?

**Economic Feasibility**

Is it financially worthwhile?

Example:

Cost vs savings.

**Operational Feasibility**

Can users adopt it?

**Legal Feasibility**

Is it compliant?

**9. Risk Analysis**

BA identifies:

-   What can go wrong?

-   Probability?

-   Impact?

-   Mitigation?

Risk Matrix:

  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Risk**            **Probability**   **Impact**   **Action**
  ------------------- ----------------- ------------ ------------------------
  AI inaccurate       Medium            High         Human review

  Data breach         Low               High         Security controls
  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

**10. Complete BA Analysis Example**

**Problem:**

Pharmacovigilance team processing is slow.

**Gap Analysis:**

Current:

Manual review

Future:

AI-assisted review

**RCA:**

Root cause:

Information scattered across systems.

**Impact Analysis:**

AI integration affects:

-   Workflow

-   Training

-   Security

**Decision Matrix:**

Choose:

Build vs Buy AI solution.

**Risk Analysis:**

AI errors.

Mitigation:

Human validation.

**11. Interview Questions**

**Q1: What is Gap Analysis?**

Answer:

\"Gap Analysis compares the current state with the desired future state
and identifies improvements required to achieve the target state.\"

**Q2: Difference between symptom and root cause?**

Answer:

\"A symptom is the visible problem, while the root cause is the
underlying reason creating that problem.\"

**Q3: Why perform Impact Analysis?**

Answer:

\"Impact Analysis helps understand the consequences of changes before
implementation and reduces unexpected issues.\"

**Practical Assignment**

For AI Pharmacovigilance Copilot:

Perform:

**1. Gap Analysis**

Create:

AS-IS vs TO-BE table

**2. RCA**

Problem:

\"Reviewers are slow in processing cases.\"

Use:

5 Whys

**3. Impact Analysis**

Change:

\"Add AI risk prediction.\"

Analyze:

-   User impact

-   Process impact

-   Technical impact

-   Risk impact

**Lesson Summary**

You learned:

✅ SWOT Analysis\
✅ Gap Analysis\
✅ Root Cause Analysis\
✅ Fishbone Diagram\
✅ Impact Analysis\
✅ Decision Matrix\
✅ MoSCoW Prioritization\
✅ Feasibility Analysis\
✅ Risk Analysis

**Lesson 28: Business Case & Product Value Analysis**

**Proving Why a Solution Should Be Built**

**A BA does not only define what to build. A senior BA helps answer:
\"Why should we invest in this solution?\"**

In enterprise projects, every idea competes for:

-   Budget

-   Resources

-   Development time

-   Management attention

A business case helps stakeholders decide:

\"Is this problem important enough to solve, and is this solution worth
investing in?\"

**Learning Objectives**

By the end of this lesson, you will understand:

-   What is a business case

-   Business case vs BRD

-   Problem statement creation

-   Opportunity assessment

-   Value proposition

-   Cost-benefit analysis

-   ROI calculation

-   MVP justification

-   Healthcare AI business case example

**1. What is a Business Case?**

Definition:

A business case is a document that explains the reason for an
initiative, expected benefits, costs, risks, and justification for
investment.

Simple:

It answers:

-   Why do this project?

-   What value will it create?

-   How much will it cost?

-   Is it worth doing?

**2. Business Case vs BRD**

Very important BA interview question.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Business Case**              **BRD**
  ------------------------------ ----------------------------------------
  Why invest?                    What business needs

  Justification                  Requirements

  Financial value                Business objectives

  Before approval                After approval
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

Example:

**Business Case:**

\"We should build AI Pharmacovigilance Platform because it can reduce
processing costs by 40%.\"

**BRD:**

\"The business requires automated case extraction and review workflow.\"

**3. Business Case Structure**

A professional BA business case contains:

**1. Executive Summary**

Short overview.

Example:

\"The company proposes an AI-assisted pharmacovigilance platform to
reduce manual case processing effort and improve regulatory
compliance.\"

**2. Problem Statement**

Clearly define current pain.

Bad:

\"Process is inefficient.\"

Good:

\"Safety reviewers spend 60% of their time manually extracting
information from adverse event reports, causing delays in regulatory
submission.\"

**3. Current Situation (AS-IS)**

Example:

Current process:

Receive Report

↓

Manual Reading

↓

Data Entry

↓

Review

↓

Submission

Problems:

-   Slow processing

-   Human errors

-   Duplicate work

**4. Opportunity Assessment**

Opportunity means:

\"What improvement is possible?\"

Example:

AI can:

-   Extract information automatically

-   Summarize reports

-   Prioritize risks

**5. Proposed Solution**

Example:

AI Pharmacovigilance Copilot:

Capabilities:

-   Document processing

-   AI summary generation

-   Risk prediction

-   Reviewer assistance

**6. Business Objectives**

Objectives should be measurable.

Example:

Before:

Case review:

5 days

Target:

2 days

Objective:

Reduce processing time by 60%.

**7. Value Proposition**

Definition:

Value proposition explains why the solution is valuable and what benefit
it provides.

Template:

\"For \[user\], our solution provides \[benefit\] by \[capability\].\"

Example:

\"For pharmacovigilance reviewers, AI Copilot reduces manual analysis
effort by automatically extracting critical safety information.\"

**8. Cost-Benefit Analysis**

A project has:

**Costs**

Money/resources required.

Examples:

-   AI development

-   Cloud infrastructure

-   Training

-   Maintenance

**Benefits**

Value gained.

Examples:

-   Reduced manual work

-   Faster processing

-   Better compliance

-   Fewer errors

**Example**

**Costs:**

AI platform development:

₹50 lakh

Annual maintenance:

₹10 lakh

**Benefits:**

Reduced manual effort:

₹80 lakh/year

Net Benefit:

₹80 lakh - ₹10 lakh

= ₹70 lakh/year

**9. ROI Calculation**

ROI:

Return on Investment

Formula:

Example:

Investment:

₹50 lakh

Annual benefit:

₹100 lakh

ROI:

= 100%

Meaning:

Every ₹1 invested returns ₹2.

**10. Payback Period**

Definition:

Time required to recover the investment.

Formula:

Example:

Investment:

₹50 lakh

Annual saving:

₹25 lakh

Payback:

2 years

**11. Risk vs Reward Analysis**

Every project has risks.

Example:

AI Solution

Benefits:

✅ Faster processing\
✅ Reduced workload

Risks:

❌ AI errors\
❌ Regulatory concerns

Mitigation:

Human-in-the-loop approval.

**12. MVP Justification**

Not every feature should be built first.

Business case helps decide MVP.

Example:

**Full Vision:**

AI Pharmacovigilance Platform

Features:

-   AI extraction

-   Risk prediction

-   Voice assistant

-   Automated submission

-   Analytics

**MVP:**

Phase 1:

✅ Document upload\
✅ AI extraction\
✅ AI summary\
✅ Human approval

Why?

Fast validation with lower investment.

**13. Opportunity Assessment Framework**

A BA evaluates:

**Market Opportunity**

Is there demand?

Example:

Healthcare AI adoption increasing.

**User Need**

Is there a real problem?

Example:

Reviewers overloaded.

**Business Value**

Does it improve outcomes?

Example:

Reduce processing time.

**Feasibility**

Can we build it?

Example:

AI models available.

**14. Business Case Example: AI Pharmacovigilance Copilot**

**Problem**

Drug safety teams manually process thousands of reports.

**Current Impact**

-   Slow review

-   High operational cost

-   Compliance pressure

**Proposed Solution**

AI assistant for:

-   Extraction

-   Summarization

-   Risk identification

**Expected Benefits**

Operational:

-   Faster review

Financial:

-   Reduced processing cost

Quality:

-   Improved consistency

Compliance:

-   Better tracking

**Investment**

Development:

₹1 crore

**Expected Annual Benefit**

₹2 crore

**ROI:**

100%

**Recommendation:**

Proceed with MVP.

**15. BA Role in Business Case**

A BA contributes by:

✅ Defining problem\
✅ Gathering data\
✅ Quantifying benefits\
✅ Identifying stakeholders\
✅ Analyzing options\
✅ Supporting decision making

**16. Interview Questions**

**Q1: What is a business case?**

Answer:

\"A business case justifies an initiative by explaining the problem,
expected benefits, costs, risks, and value to the organization.\"

**Q2: Difference between BRD and business case?**

Answer:

\"A business case explains why an initiative should be undertaken, while
a BRD defines the business requirements after the initiative is
approved.\"

**Q3: How do you measure business value?**

Answer:

\"Business value can be measured through financial benefits, cost
reduction, revenue increase, efficiency improvement, customer
satisfaction, and risk reduction.\"

**Practical Assignment**

Create a Business Case for:

**AI Pharmacovigilance Copilot**

Include:

**1. Problem Statement**

**2. Current State**

**3. Proposed Solution**

**4. Benefits**

**5. Costs**

**6. ROI Calculation**

**7. MVP Recommendation**

**Lesson Summary**

You learned:

✅ Business Case\
✅ BRD vs Business Case\
✅ Problem Statement\
✅ Opportunity Assessment\
✅ Value Proposition\
✅ Cost-Benefit Analysis\
✅ ROI Calculation\
✅ Payback Period\
✅ MVP Justification\
✅ Healthcare AI business case

**Lesson 29: Data Analysis Skills for Business Analysts**

**Using Data to Discover Problems, Validate Decisions, and Measure
Value**

**A modern BA is not only requirement-driven; a modern BA is
data-driven.**

In traditional BA work:

Stakeholder says:

\"Users are facing issues.\"

BA investigates.

In modern BA work:

Stakeholder says:

\"Users are facing issues.\"

BA asks:

-   How many users?

-   How frequently?

-   What is the impact?

-   What does the data show?

**Learning Objectives**

By the end of this lesson, you will understand:

-   Why BAs need data skills

-   Data-driven decision making

-   Types of business data

-   Metrics vs KPIs

-   Data analysis process

-   Data validation

-   SQL basics for BAs

-   Dashboard interpretation

-   Healthcare AI analytics examples

**1. Why Does a BA Need Data Skills?**

A BA uses data to:

✅ Identify problems\
✅ Validate assumptions\
✅ Prioritize features\
✅ Measure product success\
✅ Support business decisions

Example:

Stakeholder:

\"We need AI automation.\"

BA asks:

\"How much manual effort exists today?\"

Data:

-   50,000 cases/year

-   3 hours manual review/case

-   150,000 hours/year effort

Now the business problem is measurable.

**2. Traditional BA vs Data-Driven BA**

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Traditional BA**                      **Data-Driven BA**
  --------------------------------------- -------------------------------
  Relies on opinions                      Uses evidence

  Documents requirements                  Analyzes trends

  Focuses on process                      Measures outcomes

  Reactive                                Predictive
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**3. Types of Data a BA Works With**

**1. Operational Data**

Generated during business activities.

Example:

Pharmacovigilance:

-   Number of cases

-   Processing time

-   Reviewer workload

**2. Customer/User Data**

Shows user behavior.

Example:

-   Login frequency

-   Feature usage

-   Drop-off points

**3. Financial Data**

Example:

-   Cost

-   Revenue

-   Savings

**4. Product Data**

Example:

AI platform:

-   AI accuracy

-   Response time

-   Usage metrics

**4. Data Analysis Process**

A BA follows:

Business Question

↓

Collect Data

↓

Clean Data

↓

Analyze

↓

Find Insights

↓

Recommend Action

**Example**

Business Question:

\"Why are case reviews delayed?\"

Collect:

Case processing data.

Analyze:

Average processing time by team.

Insight:

80% delay occurs during medical review.

Action:

Improve medical review workflow.

**5. Metrics vs KPIs**

Very important concept.

**Metric**

Any measurable value.

Example:

Number of cases processed.

**KPI**

Key Performance Indicator.

A metric linked to business goal.

Example:

Reduce case processing time by 50%.

Comparison:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Metric**                          **KPI**
  ----------------------------------- -----------------------------------
  Measures activity                   Measures success

  Many possible                       Few important

  Operational                         Strategic
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**6. Common BA KPIs**

**Process KPIs**

Examples:

-   Processing time

-   Error rate

-   Completion rate

**Product KPIs**

Examples:

-   User adoption

-   Feature usage

-   Retention

**Business KPIs**

Examples:

-   Revenue

-   Cost savings

-   ROI

**7. Healthcare AI KPI Examples**

**AI Pharmacovigilance Platform**

**Efficiency KPIs**

Before AI:

Case review:

5 hours

After AI:

2 hours

KPI:

60% reduction in review time.

**Quality KPIs**

AI extraction accuracy:

95%

**Adoption KPIs**

Percentage of reviewers using AI:

85%

**Compliance KPIs**

Submission delays reduced:

70%

**8. Data Validation**

A BA must ensure data quality.

Before analysis ask:

**Is data complete?**

Example:

Missing case dates.

**Is data accurate?**

Example:

Wrong patient age.

**Is data consistent?**

Example:

Different drug names.

**Is data current?**

Example:

Old reports.

**9. Data Validation Example**

Problem:

\"AI accuracy is low.\"

BA checks:

Data:

10,000 cases

Finds:

3,000 cases missing important fields.

Conclusion:

Problem is not AI model.

Problem:

Poor input data quality.

**10. SQL Skills for Business Analysts**

A BA does not need to become a database developer.

But basic SQL is valuable.

SQL helps answer:

-   How many records exist?

-   What trends exist?

-   Which users perform actions?

**Basic SQL Concepts**

**SELECT**

Retrieve data.

Example:

SELECT \*

FROM cases;

Meaning:

Show all cases.

**WHERE**

Filter data.

Example:

SELECT \*

FROM cases

WHERE risk=\'HIGH\';

Meaning:

Show high-risk cases.

**COUNT**

Count records.

Example:

SELECT COUNT(\*)

FROM cases;

Meaning:

Total cases.

**GROUP BY**

Analyze categories.

Example:

SELECT status, COUNT(\*)

FROM cases

GROUP BY status;

Meaning:

Cases by status.

**11. BA SQL Example**

Question:

\"How many serious cases are pending?\"

Query:

SELECT COUNT(\*)

FROM cases

WHERE severity=\'SERIOUS\'

AND status=\'PENDING\';

Business Insight:

\"250 serious cases require attention.\"

**12. Data Visualization**

BAs often work with dashboards.

Common tools:

-   Microsoft Power BI

-   Tableau

BA uses dashboards to understand:

-   Trends

-   Patterns

-   Problems

-   Performance

**13. Dashboard Example**

AI Pharmacovigilance Dashboard:

\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--

Total Cases

50,000

Pending Review

2,500

Average Processing Time

2.5 days

AI Accuracy

94%

\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\-\--

BA Insight:

\"Pending cases increased 30% after new drug launch.\"

Recommendation:

Increase reviewer capacity.

**14. Data Analysis for Prioritization**

Example:

Feature requests:

1.  AI Summary

2.  Voice Assistant

3.  Dark Mode

Data:

Users spend 70% time reading documents.

Decision:

Prioritize AI Summary.

Data prevents opinion-based decisions.

**15. Data Analysis + Product Management**

This connects BA with Product roles.

Product question:

\"Should we build this feature?\"

BA analyzes:

-   User behavior

-   Business impact

-   Cost

-   Expected value

**16. Interview Questions**

**Q1: Why does a BA need data analysis skills?**

Answer:

\"Data analysis helps BAs validate requirements, identify problems,
measure outcomes, and support evidence-based decision making.\"

**Q2: Difference between metric and KPI?**

Answer:

\"A metric is any measurable value, while a KPI is a critical metric
directly linked to business objectives.\"

**Q3: How do you validate data?**

Answer:

\"I check completeness, accuracy, consistency, validity, and timeliness
before using data for analysis.\"

**Practical Assignment**

For AI Pharmacovigilance Copilot:

Create a KPI Dashboard.

Include:

**Operational KPIs**

-   Cases processed

-   Average review time

-   Pending cases

**AI KPIs**

-   Extraction accuracy

-   Confidence score

-   AI adoption rate

**Business KPIs**

-   Cost reduction

-   Time saved

-   ROI

**Lesson Summary**

You learned:

✅ Importance of data skills for BA\
✅ Data-driven decision making\
✅ Metrics vs KPIs\
✅ Data validation\
✅ SQL basics\
✅ Dashboard analysis\
✅ Healthcare AI analytics\
✅ Product decision support

**Jira, Confluence, Miro, Figma, SQL, Power BI & Real BA Workflow**

**A Business Analyst is not only expected to understand requirements but
also to work efficiently using industry tools.**

In modern Agile teams, a BA spends the day moving between:

-   Requirement documentation

-   Backlog management

-   Process modelling

-   Stakeholder workshops

-   Prototype reviews

-   Data analysis

-   Reporting

Tools help a BA convert ideas into executable work.

**Learning Objectives**

By the end of this lesson, you will understand:

-   BA tool ecosystem

**1. BA Tool Ecosystem**

A typical BA tool stack:

Business Need

↓

Miro

(Process Discovery)

↓

Confluence

(Business Documentation)

↓

Jira

(User Stories & Backlog)

↓

Figma

(UI Validation)

↓

SQL + Power BI

(Data Analysis)

↓

Testing & Delivery

**2. Jira for Business Analysts**

Jira is one of the most widely used Agile tools.

A BA uses Jira for:

-   User stories

-   Requirements tracking

-   Backlog refinement

-   Acceptance criteria

-   Sprint planning

-   Defect tracking

**Jira Work Items**

Common Jira items:

**Epic**

Large business capability.

Example:

AI Pharmacovigilance Platform

**Story**

User requirement.

Example:

As a reviewer,

I want AI summary generation,

so that I can review cases faster.

**Task**

Technical activity.

Example:

Integrate NLP model API

**Bug**

Defect.

Example:

AI confidence score not displayed

**Jira Hierarchy**

Epic

↓

Feature

↓

User Story

↓

Task / Sub-task

↓

Bug

**3. BA Activities in Jira**

**Creating User Stories**

BA creates:

-   Title

-   Description

-   Acceptance Criteria

-   Priority

-   Dependencies

Example:

Jira Story:

Title:

Generate AI Case Summary

Description:

As a safety reviewer,

I want AI-generated summaries,

so that I can reduce review time.

Acceptance Criteria:

Given a document is uploaded

When AI processing completes

Then summary should be displayed.

**Backlog Management**

BA helps:

-   Remove duplicate stories

-   Split large stories

-   Clarify requirements

-   Add missing acceptance criteria

**Sprint Refinement Example**

Developer asks:

\"What happens when AI confidence is below 70%?\"

BA updates:

Business Rule:

If confidence \<70%,

manual review required.

**4. Confluence for BA Documentation**

Confluence is used for documentation.

BA maintains:

-   BRD

-   FRD

-   Meeting notes

-   Process documents

-   Decision logs

-   Requirement repository

**Example Confluence Structure**

Healthcare AI Project

│

├── Business Requirements

│

├── Process Models

│

├── User Stories

│

├── Meeting Notes

│

├── Decisions

│

└── UAT Documents

**BA Best Practice**

Avoid storing requirements in emails.

Use centralized documentation.

**5. Miro for Business Analysis**

Miro is a collaboration tool.

BA uses Miro for:

-   Workshops

-   Brainstorming

-   Journey mapping

-   Process mapping

-   Affinity mapping

**Example: User Journey Mapping**

AI Pharmacovigilance User Journey:

Receive Report

↓

Upload Document

↓

AI Extraction

↓

Reviewer Validation

↓

Risk Assessment

↓

Submission

**Miro Workshop Example**

Participants add sticky notes:

Problems:

-   Manual entry

-   Duplicate work

-   Missing information

BA groups similar problems.

**6. Figma for BA Collaboration**

Figma is mainly a design tool.

BA uses it for:

-   Wireframe review

-   Prototype feedback

-   User flow validation

Example:

Product team creates:

AI Dashboard Prototype

BA validates:

-   Does workflow match requirements?

-   Are all user actions covered?

-   Are error states included?

**7. SQL Tools for BA**

Common SQL environments:

-   Database clients

-   Cloud query tools

-   Data warehouses

BA uses SQL to answer business questions.

Example:

Question:

\"How many high-risk cases are pending?\"

Query:

SELECT COUNT(\*)

FROM cases

WHERE risk=\'HIGH\'

AND status=\'PENDING\';

BA uses results for:

-   Reports

-   Decisions

-   Requirements validation

**8. Power BI for BA**

Microsoft Power BI helps create dashboards.

BA uses it for:

-   KPI tracking

-   Trend analysis

-   Executive reporting

Example Dashboard:

AI Pharmacovigilance Metrics:

Total Cases 50,000

AI Accuracy 95%

Average Review 2 Days

Pending Cases 1,200

Cost Saved ₹80L

**Q1: How do you use Jira as a BA?**

Answer:

\"I use Jira to create and manage user stories, define acceptance
criteria, support backlog refinement, track requirements, and
collaborate with Agile teams.\"

**Q2: Why use Confluence?**

Answer:

\"Confluence provides a centralized repository for requirements,
decisions, documentation, and project knowledge.\"

**Q3: How do you validate a prototype?**

Answer:

\"I compare the prototype with business requirements, user journeys,
workflows, and acceptance criteria to ensure it solves the intended
problem.\"
