Today you learned:

✅ Backlog refinement concept\
✅ BA role in refinement\
✅ Definition of Ready\
✅ Story splitting\
✅ Dependency identification\
✅ Healthcare AI refinement example\
✅ Refinement meeting process

**PHASE 6 -- Agile BA Execution**

**Lesson 29 -- Sprint Planning Collaboration Masterclass**

**How a BA Supports Sprint Planning and Sprint Execution**

**Module Progress Reminder**

Your roadmap:

**Total Lessons:** 36

Completed:

✅ Lesson 1--28

Current:

➡️ **Lesson 29 of 36**

Remaining:

**7 Lessons Left**

**1. What is Sprint Planning?**

**Definition**

Sprint Planning is a Scrum event where the team decides what work will
be completed during the upcoming sprint and how that work will be
achieved.

Simple:

Sprint Planning answers two questions:

**Question 1:**

**What are we going to build?**

(Product Backlog selection)

**Question 2:**

**How will we build it?**

(Technical approach and tasks)

**2. Sprint Planning Inputs**

Before Sprint Planning begins, the team needs:

✅ Product Backlog\
✅ Prioritized User Stories\
✅ Acceptance Criteria\
✅ Definition of Ready\
✅ Product Goal\
✅ Team Capacity

This is where BA preparation becomes important.

**3. Sprint Planning Participants**

Scrum Team:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Role**             **Responsibility**
  -------------------- --------------------------------------------------
  Product Owner        Explains priority and business value

  BA                   Clarifies requirements and workflows

  Scrum Master         Facilitates meeting

  Developers           Estimate and plan implementation
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**4. BA Role Before Sprint Planning**

A BA prepares the team.

**1. Ensure Stories Are Ready**

BA checks:

-   User story exists

-   Acceptance criteria defined

-   Business rules documented

-   Dependencies identified

Example:

Before:

\"Improve AI review process.\"

Not ready.

After:

\"As a safety reviewer, I want AI risk scores so that I can prioritize
serious cases.\"

Ready.

**2. Validate Business Priority**

BA works with Product Owner.

Example:

Priority:

High:

AI risk classification

Medium:

Dashboard customization

Low:

Export formatting

**3. Clarify Dependencies**

Example:

Story:

AI risk prediction

Dependencies:

-   AI model

-   Historical safety data

-   Drug database API

**5. BA Role During Sprint Planning**

**Activity 1:**

**Explain User Stories**

BA explains:

-   User problem

-   Expected behavior

-   Business value

Example:

Story:

AI Summary Generation

BA explains:

\"Reviewers spend 30 minutes reading each report. AI summary should
reduce review time.\"

**Activity 2:**

**Answer Requirement Questions**

Developer:

\"Should users edit AI summaries?\"

BA:

\"Yes, reviewers can edit before approval.\"

**Activity 3:**

**Clarify Acceptance Criteria**

Example:

Acceptance:

\"Risk score should be displayed.\"

Developer asks:

\"Where?\"

BA clarifies:

\"On case details page above medical review section.\"

**Activity 4:**

**Identify Missing Scenarios**

BA asks:

\"What happens when AI confidence is low?\"

Decision:

Low confidence cases require manual review.

**6. Sprint Planning Structure**

Typical flow:

Sprint Planning

↓

Review Product Goal

↓

Select Stories

↓

Discuss Requirements

↓

Estimate Capacity

↓

Create Sprint Goal

↓

Break Stories into Tasks

**7. Sprint Goal**

A sprint should have a clear goal.

Example:

Bad:

\"Complete five stories.\"

Good:

\"Enable safety reviewers to automatically analyze and prioritize
adverse event cases.\"

Why?

It focuses on value, not only tasks.

**8. Story Selection Example**

**Product Backlog**

AI Pharmacovigilance Platform

Top Items:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Story**                                            **Priority**
  ---------------------------------------------------- ------------------
  Upload safety reports                                High

  AI summary generation                                High

  Dashboard filters                                    Medium

  User profile customization                           Low
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

Sprint Capacity:

2 weeks

Team selects:

1.  Upload safety reports

2.  AI summary generation

3.  Dashboard basic view

**9. BA Role in Sprint Scope Discussion**

Sometimes teams cannot complete everything.

BA helps decide:

What is essential?

Example:

AI Summary Feature:

Must Have:

✅ Generate summary

Can Wait:

❌ Summary formatting options

**10. Sprint Planning Example -- Healthcare AI**

**Sprint Goal:**

\"Enable AI-assisted safety case analysis.\"

Selected Stories:

**Story 1:**

As a reviewer, I want to upload adverse event documents.

Acceptance:

-   PDF accepted

-   Document stored

-   Case created

**Story 2:**

As a reviewer, I want AI-generated summaries.

Acceptance:

-   Summary generated

-   Key information displayed

-   Reviewer can edit

**Story 3:**

As a reviewer, I want risk scores.

Acceptance:

-   Score displayed

-   Category shown

-   Explanation available

**11. BA During Sprint Execution**

Sprint planning is not the end.

BA continues support.

Daily Activities:

-   Answer questions

-   Clarify requirements

-   Update stories if needed

-   Coordinate with stakeholders

Example:

Developer:

\"Business wants risk score visible?\"

BA:

\"Yes, this is a mandatory acceptance criteria.\"

**12. Sprint Planning Mistakes**

**Mistake 1:**

Taking unclear stories into sprint.

**Mistake 2:**

Missing acceptance criteria.

**Mistake 3:**

Ignoring technical dependencies.

**Mistake 4:**

Selecting too much work.

**Mistake 5:**

BA disappears after sprint starts.

**13. Sprint Planning vs Backlog Refinement**

Interview question.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Backlog Refinement**          **Sprint Planning**
  ------------------------------- ---------------------------------------
  Prepare future work             Select current sprint work

  Continuous activity             Beginning of sprint

  Clarify stories                 Commit sprint goal

  Estimate readiness              Plan execution
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**14. BA Deliverables Related to Sprint Planning**

BA may provide:

✅ User stories\
✅ Acceptance criteria\
✅ Process flows\
✅ Business rules\
✅ Requirement notes\
✅ Data requirements\
✅ Decision logs

**15. Interview Questions**

**Q1. What is BA\'s role in Sprint Planning?**

Answer:

\"BA helps ensure selected stories are clear, explains business
requirements, clarifies acceptance criteria, identifies dependencies,
and supports the team during planning.\"

**Q2. Does BA decide sprint priority?**

Answer:

\"Usually the Product Owner owns priority, while BA supports with
analysis and business understanding.\"

**Q3. What happens if requirements are unclear during Sprint Planning?**

Answer:

\"The BA clarifies requirements, updates acceptance criteria, or helps
the team decide whether the story is ready.\"

**Practical Assignment**

For AI Pharmacovigilance Platform:

Create a Sprint Planning document.

**Sprint Goal:**

\"Improve AI-assisted safety review.\"

Include:

1.  Sprint Goal

2.  Three selected user stories

3.  Acceptance criteria

4.  Dependencies

5.  BA responsibilities

**PHASE 6 -- Agile BA Execution**

**Lesson 30 -- UAT Planning & Execution Masterclass**

**How a BA Ensures the Product Solves the Real Business Need**

**Module Progress Reminder**

Your roadmap:

**Total Lessons:** 36

Completed:

✅ Lesson 1--29

Current:

➡️ **Lesson 30 of 36**

Remaining:

**6 Lessons Left**

**1. What is UAT?**

**Definition**

User Acceptance Testing (UAT) is the process where business users
validate that the developed solution meets their business needs and is
ready for real-world use.

Simple:

Testing team asks:

\"Does the system work correctly?\"

Business users ask:

\"Does this system solve my problem?\"

UAT answers:

\"Can the business accept this product?\"

**2. Where Does UAT Come in Software Lifecycle?**

Requirement

↓

Design

↓

Development

↓

System Testing (QA)

↓

UAT

↓

Business Approval

↓

Production Release

**3. Why is UAT Important?**

A system can pass technical testing but fail business expectations.

Example:

Developer builds AI risk dashboard.

QA confirms:

✅ Button works\
✅ Data loads\
✅ No errors

But business discovers:

❌ Risk score explanation is missing\
❌ Workflow does not match reviewer process

UAT catches these issues.

**4. BA Role in UAT**

The BA is the bridge between:

Business Users ↔ Development Team

BA responsibilities:

1.  Plan UAT

2.  Define scenarios

3.  Prepare test data

4.  Coordinate users

5.  Manage defects

6.  Collect approval

**5. UAT Planning Process**

A professional BA follows:

Understand Business Goal

↓

Identify UAT Participants

↓

Create UAT Strategy

↓

Prepare Test Scenarios

↓

Prepare Test Data

↓

Execute Testing

↓

Record Defects

↓

Business Sign-off

**6. UAT Participants**

Example:

AI Pharmacovigilance Platform

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **User**                   **Responsibility**
  -------------------------- --------------------------------------------
  Safety Reviewer            Validate case review process

  Medical Reviewer           Validate approval workflow

  Compliance Officer         Validate regulatory requirements

  Product Owner              Final acceptance
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**7. UAT Strategy Document**

A BA may prepare:

**UAT Plan**

Contains:

**1. Objective**

Example:

\"Validate that AI-assisted case review meets pharmacovigilance team
requirements.\"

**2. Scope**

**In Scope:**

✅ Case upload\
✅ AI summary\
✅ Risk classification\
✅ Approval workflow

**Out of Scope:**

❌ AI model training\
❌ Database optimization

**3. Test Participants**

Safety reviewers and medical reviewers.

**4. Test Environment**

Example:

UAT environment with production-like data.

**5. Entry Criteria**

Conditions before UAT starts.

Example:

-   Development completed

-   QA testing completed

-   Environment ready

**6. Exit Criteria**

Conditions for completion.

Example:

-   All critical defects closed

-   Business approval received

**8. Creating UAT Test Scenarios**

A BA converts requirements into business scenarios.

Example User Story:

As a safety reviewer, I want AI-generated summaries so that I can review
cases faster.

UAT Scenario:

**Scenario:**

Generate AI Case Summary

**Steps:**

1.  Login as reviewer

2.  Upload adverse event report

3.  Start AI analysis

4.  View generated summary

**Expected Result:**

System displays:

-   Patient details

-   Drug information

-   Event details

-   Risk information

**9. UAT Test Case Template**

Industry format:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Field**                       **Example**
  ------------------------------- ---------------------------------------
  Test ID                         UAT-001

  Requirement                     AI Summary

  Scenario                        Generate summary

  Preconditions                   User logged in

  Test Steps                      Upload report

  Expected Result                 Summary displayed

  Actual Result                   Filled during testing

  Status                          Pass/Fail
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**10. UAT Example -- Healthcare AI System**

**Feature:**

AI Risk Classification

**Test Case:**

ID:

UAT-AI-002

Requirement:

System should classify safety case risk.

Steps:

1.  Open completed case.

2.  Select AI analysis.

3.  View risk score.

Expected:

System displays:

-   Risk category

-   Confidence score

-   Explanation

Result:

Pass / Fail

**11. Negative UAT Scenarios**

Good BA always tests exceptions.

Example:

**Scenario:**

Invalid document upload

Steps:

1.  Upload unsupported file

Expected:

System displays:

\"Unsupported format. Please upload PDF or XML.\"

Other negative cases:

-   Missing mandatory fields

-   Unauthorized user

-   AI processing failure

-   Network interruption

**12. UAT Defect Management**

During UAT defects are found.

BA helps classify them.

Defect Severity:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Severity**      **Meaning**
  ----------------- -----------------------------------------------------
  Critical          Business cannot continue

  High              Major functionality broken

  Medium            Workaround exists

  Low               Minor issue
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

Example:

Critical:

AI gives wrong regulatory classification.

Low:

Dashboard alignment issue.

**13. UAT vs QA Testing**

Important interview topic.

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **QA Testing**                **UAT**
  ----------------------------- -----------------------------------------
  Technical validation          Business validation

  QA team                       Business users

  Checks defects                Checks business acceptance

  System behavior               Business outcome
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**14. UAT Sign-off**

After successful testing:

Business provides approval.

Example:

\"The AI Pharmacovigilance Platform meets business requirements and is
approved for production deployment.\"

Sign-off usually includes:

-   Date

-   Version tested

-   Approved features

-   Known limitations

-   Stakeholder approval

**15. Common BA Mistakes in UAT**

**Mistake 1:**

Starting UAT without clear scenarios.

**Mistake 2:**

Testing only positive cases.

**Mistake 3:**

No business users involved.

**Mistake 4:**

Ignoring defects until release.

**Mistake 5:**

No formal sign-off.

**16. Interview Questions**

**Q1. What is UAT?**

Answer:

\"UAT is business validation testing where end users verify that the
solution meets their requirements before production release.\"

**Q2. What is BA\'s role in UAT?**

Answer:

\"BA prepares UAT scenarios, coordinates users, manages defects,
clarifies requirements, and supports business sign-off.\"

**Q3. Difference between QA and UAT?**

Answer:

\"QA validates whether the system works correctly, while UAT validates
whether the system solves the business need.\"

1.  UAT Objective

2.  Participants

3.  Three UAT Scenarios

4.  Test Cases

6.  Defect Severity Rules

✅ UAT planning\
✅ UAT scenarios\
✅ Test cases\

**PHASE 6 -- Agile BA Execution**

**Lesson 31 -- Defect Management Masterclass**

**How a BA Handles Defects from Discovery to Resolution**

**Module Progress Reminder**

Your roadmap:

**Total Lessons:** 36

Completed:

✅ Lesson 1--30

Current:

➡️ **Lesson 31 of 36**

Remaining:

**5 Lessons Left**

**1. What is a Defect?**

**Definition**

A defect is a gap between the expected behavior of a system and its
actual behavior.

Simple:

Expected:

\"The system should show AI risk score.\"

Actual:

\"Risk score is missing.\"

This difference is a **defect**.

**2. Defect Terminology**

Important BA interview topic.

**Bug**

A coding error that causes incorrect behavior.

Example:

Developer calculation mistake.

**Defect**

Any deviation from expected requirement.

Example:

Feature does not meet acceptance criteria.

**Issue**

Any problem affecting project delivery.

Example:

Missing test data.

**Error**

Human mistake during development.

Relationship:

Human Mistake

↓

Error

↓

Creates Bug

↓

Observed as Defect

↓

Reported as Issue

**3. Defect Lifecycle**

A professional defect follows a lifecycle:

id=\"x3j9cd\"

New

↓

Assigned

↓

Analysis

↓

Fixed

↓

Retest

↓

Closed

Detailed:

**1. New**

QA/business identifies problem.

**2. Assigned**

Defect assigned to responsible team.

**3. Analysis**

Developer investigates cause.

**4. Fixed**

Code correction completed.

**5. Retest**

QA verifies fix.

**6. Closed**

Defect confirmed resolved.

**4. Defect Lifecycle Example**

Feature:

AI Risk Classification

Problem:

High-risk case shown as Medium risk.

Lifecycle:

**New:**

QA reports defect.

↓

**Assigned:**

Developer receives issue.

↓

**Analysis:**

AI scoring rule incorrect.

↓

**Fixed:**

Rule corrected.

↓

**Retest:**

QA verifies.

↓

**Closed:**

Business accepts.

**5. Severity vs Priority**

Very important interview topic.

**Severity**

**Meaning:**

How badly does the defect impact the system?

Usually decided by:

QA + Technical team

**Priority**

**Meaning:**

How quickly should the defect be fixed?

Usually decided by:

Product Owner + Business

Comparison:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Severity**                       **Priority**
  ---------------------------------- ------------------------------------
  Technical impact                   Business urgency

  How bad                            How soon

  QA focus                           Business focus
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**Examples:**

**Example 1:**

Critical Severity + High Priority

AI generates incorrect regulatory decision.

Impact:

Patient safety risk.

**Example 2:**

Low Severity + High Priority

Company logo incorrect before major launch.

Business importance high.

**Example 3:**

High Severity + Low Priority

Rare report export failure not used currently.

**6. Defect Classification**

Common categories:

**Functional Defect**

Feature does not work.

Example:

Button does not submit case.

**Data Defect**

Incorrect data.

Example:

Wrong drug information displayed.

**UI Defect**

Visual issue.

Example:

Dashboard alignment problem.

**Performance Defect**

System slow.

Example:

AI summary takes 10 minutes.

**Security Defect**

Unauthorized access.

Example:

Reviewer can see restricted cases.

**7. BA Role in Defect Management**

BA is not the person fixing bugs.

BA ensures:

-   Correct understanding

-   Business impact analysis

-   Requirement clarification

**BA Responsibility 1:**

**Validate Defect Against Requirement**

Example:

Defect:

\"Risk score missing.\"

BA checks:

Acceptance Criteria:

\"Risk score must be displayed.\"

Confirmed: Valid defect.

**BA Responsibility 2:**

**Support Defect Triage**

Defect triage meeting:

Participants:

-   BA

-   Product Owner

-   Developer

-   QA

-   Scrum Master

Questions:

1.  Is this really a defect?

2.  How serious is it?

3.  Should it be fixed now?

4.  What is business impact?

**BA Responsibility 3:**

**Analyze Business Impact**

Example:

Defect:

AI confidence score not visible.

Impact:

Reviewers cannot judge AI reliability.

Decision:

High priority fix.

**BA Responsibility 4:**

**Update Requirements**

Sometimes defects reveal missing requirements.

Example:

Problem:

System does not handle incomplete reports.

BA adds:

Business Rule:

\"System shall notify users when mandatory information is missing.\"

**8. Defect Report Template**

Professional format:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Field**           **Example**
  ------------------- ---------------------------------------------------
  Defect ID           DEF-001

  Title               Wrong AI Risk Score

  Description         High risk case classified incorrectly

  Environment         UAT

  Steps               Open case → Run analysis

  Expected            High risk

  Actual              Medium risk

  Severity            Critical

  Priority            High

  Status              Open
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**9. Defect Example -- AI Pharmacovigilance Platform**

**Defect:**

AI Risk Classification Error

**Description:**

The system classifies a serious adverse event as low risk.

**Expected:**

High-risk category.

**Actual:**

Low-risk category.

**Business Impact:**

Could delay regulatory review.

**Severity:**

Critical

**Priority:**

High

**Root Cause:**

Incorrect AI classification rule.

**10. Defect Triage Process**

Typical flow:

Defect Reported

↓

Review Impact

↓

Validate Requirement

↓

Assign Severity/Priority

↓

Decide Fix Timeline

↓

Track Resolution

**11. Defect Management Tools**

Common industry tools:

-   Jira

-   Azure DevOps

-   ServiceNow

BA uses these tools to:

-   Review defects

-   Add clarification

-   Link defects to stories

-   Track status

**12. Defect Metrics**

Agile teams track:

**Defect Count**

Number of defects found.

**Defect Leakage**

Defects escaping into production.

Formula:

Production Defects /

Total Defects

**Defect Resolution Time**

How quickly defects are fixed.

**Reopen Rate**

How many defects return after fixing.

**13. Root Cause Analysis of Defects**

Not every defect should only be fixed.

BA asks:

\"Why did this happen?\"

Example:

Problem:

Wrong risk score.

Why?

↓

AI rule incorrect.

Why?

↓

Requirement did not define classification rules.

Why?

↓

Business rules missing.

Root Cause:

Incomplete requirement analysis.

**14. Common BA Mistakes**

**Mistake 1:**

Accepting every reported problem as defect.

**Mistake 2:**

Ignoring business impact.

**Mistake 3:**

No requirement traceability.

**Mistake 4:**

Fixing symptom instead of root cause.

**15. Interview Questions**

**Q1. Difference between severity and priority?**

Answer:

\"Severity measures impact of the defect, while priority determines how
quickly it should be resolved.\"

**Q2. What is BA\'s role in defect management?**

Answer:

\"BA validates defects against requirements, analyzes business impact,
supports triage, and ensures correct resolution.\"

**Q3. What is defect leakage?**

Answer:

\"Defect leakage occurs when defects are missed during testing and reach
production.\"

For AI Pharmacovigilance Platform:

Create 5 defects.

For each:

1.  Defect Title

2.  Description

3.  Expected Result

4.  Actual Result

5.  Severity

6.  Priority

7.  Business Impact

✅ Bug vs defect vs issue\
✅ Defect lifecycle\
✅ Severity vs priority\
✅ Defect triage\
✅ BA responsibilities\
✅ Root cause analysis\
✅ Healthcare AI defect examples

**Lesson 32 -- Release Documentation Masterclass**

**How a BA Supports Product Release, Deployment & Business Transition**

**Module Progress Reminder**

Your roadmap:

**Total Lessons:** 36

Completed:

✅ Lesson 1--31

Current:

➡️ **Lesson 32 of 36**

**1. What is Release Documentation?**

**Definition**

Release Documentation is a set of documents that describe what is being
released, what has changed, how users should use it, and how the
organization should transition to the new version.

Simple:

Release documentation answers:

-   What are we releasing?

-   Why are we releasing it?

-   What changed?

-   Who is impacted?

-   How should users use it?

**2. Where Does Release Documentation Come?**

Software lifecycle:

Requirement

↓

Development

↓

Testing

↓

UAT

↓

Release Preparation

↓

Production Deployment

↓

Post Release Support

Release documentation happens before production launch.

**3. Why is Release Documentation Important?**

A good release document provides:

✅ Clear communication\
✅ User awareness\
✅ Deployment readiness\
✅ Compliance evidence\
✅ Change tracking

Especially important in:

-   Healthcare

-   Banking

-   Government

-   Regulated industries

**4. BA Role in Release Management**

A BA supports:

1.  Release planning

2.  Requirement verification

3.  Business communication

4.  User documentation

5.  Training support

6.  Go-live readiness

BA does not usually deploy software.

Technical teams handle deployment.

**5. Types of Release Documents**

A BA may create or support:

**1. Release Notes**

Most common.

Explains:

-   New features

-   Improvements

-   Bug fixes

-   Known limitations

**2. User Guide**

Explains:

-   How users operate the system

**3. Training Material**

Supports adoption.

**4. Deployment Checklist**

Ensures readiness.

**5. Change Request Documentation**

Tracks approved changes.

**6. Release Plan**

Defines:

-   Timeline

-   Activities

-   Responsibilities

**6. Release Notes Structure**

Professional format:

Release Version:

Release Date:

Overview:

New Features:

Enhancements:

Bug Fixes:

Known Issues:

Impact:

**7. Release Notes Example**

**Product:**

AI Pharmacovigilance Platform

**Version:**

Release 2.0

**Overview:**

This release introduces AI-assisted safety case analysis capabilities.

**New Features:**

**1. AI Case Summary**

Users can generate automated summaries from safety reports.

**2. Risk Classification**

System provides AI-based risk categories.

**3. Reviewer Dashboard**

Users can view prioritized cases.

**Improvements:**

-   Faster document processing

-   Improved search capability

-   Better case filtering

**Bug Fixes:**

-   Fixed duplicate case display issue

-   Fixed report export error

**Known Limitations:**

-   AI recommendations require human validation.

**8. Release Readiness Checklist**

Before go-live:

**Business Readiness**

✅ UAT completed\
✅ Business approval received\
✅ Users informed

**Functional Readiness**

✅ Features tested\
✅ Defects resolved\
✅ Acceptance criteria completed

**Technical Readiness**

✅ Deployment completed\
✅ Backup available\
✅ Monitoring enabled

**Documentation Readiness**

✅ User guide ready\
✅ Release notes published\
✅ Training completed

**9. Go-Live Checklist Example**

**AI Pharmacovigilance Platform**

Before launch:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Activity**                                      **Status**
  ------------------------------------------------- ---------------------
  UAT sign-off                                      Complete

  Security validation                               Complete

  User training                                     Complete

  Data migration                                    Complete

  Production deployment                             Planned
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**10. Change Management in Releases**

A release often changes user behavior.

Example:

Old Process:

Reviewer manually reads reports.

↓

New Process:

AI generates summary first.

BA helps manage:

-   Communication

-   Training

-   Adoption issues

**11. Version Management**

Software releases use versions.

Example:

**Major Release**

2.0

Large new capabilities.

**Minor Release**

2.1

Enhancements.

**Patch Release**

2.1.1

Bug fixes.

Example:

AI Platform:

Version 1.0:

Basic case management

Version 2.0:

AI analysis added

Version 2.0.1:

Bug fixes

**12. BA Role During Go-Live**

During release:

BA supports:

**1. Business Validation**

Confirm:

\"Does released feature match expectation?\"

**2. User Support**

Answer:

-   How does feature work?

-   What changed?

**3. Issue Analysis**

If users report problems:

BA helps classify:

-   Requirement issue

-   Training issue

-   Defect

**13. Post Release Activities**

After launch:

BA helps collect:

-   User feedback

-   Improvement requests

-   New requirements

Example:

Users request:

\"Add AI explanation history.\"

BA converts:

Feedback →

Feature Request →

User Story

**14. Healthcare AI Release Example**

**Release:**

AI Risk Prediction Module

**Business Objective:**

Improve prioritization of serious safety cases.

**User Impact:**

Safety reviewers receive automated risk recommendations.

**Training Required:**

Reviewers learn:

-   How to interpret AI score

-   How to validate recommendation

**Compliance Note:**

Human approval remains mandatory.

**15. Common BA Mistakes**

**Mistake 1:**

Assuming release is only technical.

**Mistake 2:**

Ignoring user training.

**Mistake 3:**

Missing business communication.

**Mistake 4:**

No documentation update.

**16. Interview Questions**

**Q1. What is release documentation?**

Answer:

\"Release documentation describes changes included in a software
release, deployment information, user impact, and operational
guidance.\"

**Q2. What is BA\'s role during release?**

Answer:

\"BA supports release readiness by validating requirements, coordinating
UAT, preparing documentation, and helping users transition.\"

**Q3. What are release notes?**

Answer:

\"Release notes summarize new features, enhancements, bug fixes, and
known issues included in a release.\"

**Module Progress Reminder**

**1. Why Portfolio Projects Matter for a BA?**

A BA interview is not only about theory.

Companies ask:

\"Tell me about a project where you analyzed requirements, worked with
stakeholders, and delivered a solution.\"

You need a realistic project story.

For your target path:

**Pharmacologist → Healthcare AI BA/Product Analyst**

A healthcare AI project is ideal because it combines:

-   Domain knowledge

-   Business analysis

-   AI product understanding

-   Agile execution

**2. Project Case Study Introduction**

**Project Name:**

**AI Pharmacovigilance Intelligence Platform**

**Industry:**

Pharmaceutical / Life Sciences

**Business Area:**

Drug Safety Monitoring

**Project Type:**

AI-powered SaaS platform

**3. Business Background**

A pharmaceutical company receives thousands of adverse event reports
from:

-   Patients

-   Doctors

-   Hospitals

-   Regulatory authorities

Currently, safety teams manually:

-   Read reports

-   Extract information

-   Classify severity

-   Prioritize cases

-   Prepare regulatory submissions

**4. Existing Business Problem (AS-IS)**

Current process:

Adverse Event Received

↓

Manual Data Entry

↓

Safety Reviewer Analysis

↓

Medical Review

↓

Regulatory Submission

Problems:

**Problem 1:**

Large manual workload.

Example:

Reviewer spends 20--30 minutes per case.

**Problem 2:**

Delayed case processing.

**Problem 3:**

High chance of inconsistency.

**Problem 4:**

Difficulty identifying high-risk cases quickly.

**5. BA Problem Statement**

Professional BA format:

**Current Situation:**

The pharmacovigilance team manually processes adverse event reports.

**Business Problem:**

Manual review causes delays and increases operational effort.

**Business Impact:**

-   Higher operational cost

-   Delayed safety reporting

-   Increased reviewer workload

**Business Need:**

Implement AI-assisted case processing.

**6. Stakeholder Analysis**

A BA begins with stakeholders.

  ------------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Stakeholder**               **Role**            **Interest**
  ----------------------------- ------------------- ----------------------
  Head of Pharmacovigilance     Business Owner      Reduce review time

  Safety Reviewer               End User            Faster case analysis

  Medical Reviewer              Validator           Accurate assessment

  Compliance Team               Regulatory          Ensure compliance

  IT Team                       Technical           System delivery

  Product Manager               Product Direction   Roadmap
  ------------------------------------------------------------------------

**7. Stakeholder Power-Interest Matrix**

**High Power / High Interest**

Manage closely:

-   Head of Pharmacovigilance

-   Product Owner

**High Power / Low Interest**

Keep satisfied:

-   Compliance leadership

**Low Power / High Interest**

Keep informed:

-   Safety reviewers

**Low Power / Low Interest**

Monitor:

-   Supporting teams

**8. Business Objectives**

The BA defines measurable goals.

**Objective 1:**

Reduce manual case review time by 50%.

**Objective 2:**

Improve case prioritization accuracy.

**Objective 3:**

Provide audit-ready review history.

**Objective 4:**

Improve regulatory reporting efficiency.

**9. Scope Definition**

**In Scope**

✅ AI document extraction\
✅ Case summarization\
✅ Risk classification\
✅ Reviewer dashboard\
✅ Audit trail

**Out of Scope**

❌ Drug discovery\
❌ Patient diagnosis\
❌ Treatment recommendation

**10. AS-IS Process Analysis**

Current workflow:

Receive Report

↓

Manual Data Entry

↓

Medical Review

↓

Risk Assessment

↓

Regulatory Reporting

BA identifies pain points:

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Step**                            **Issue**
  ----------------------------------- -----------------------------------
  Data Entry                          Time consuming

  Review                              Manual effort

  Risk Assessment                     Inconsistent

  Reporting                           Delays
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**11. TO-BE Process Design**

Future workflow:

Report Received

↓

AI Data Extraction

↓

AI Summary Generation

↓

AI Risk Classification

↓

Human Validation

↓

Regulatory Submission

Important BA point:

AI does not replace humans.

It supports decision-making.

**12. BRD Summary**

**Business Requirement:**

The company requires an AI-powered pharmacovigilance platform to improve
safety case processing efficiency.

**Success Metrics:**

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Metric**                                 **Target**
  ------------------------------------------ ----------------------------
  Review time                                Reduce 50%

  Processing accuracy                        Improve 30%

  User adoption                              \>90%
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**13. FRD Summary**

**Functional Requirements**

**FR-001:**

System shall allow users to upload adverse event reports.

**FR-002:**

System shall extract relevant medical information.

**FR-003:**

System shall generate AI summaries.

**FR-004:**

System shall classify case risk.

**FR-005:**

System shall maintain audit history.

**14. User Stories**

**Epic:**

AI-Assisted Safety Review

**Story 1:**

As a safety reviewer,

I want AI-generated summaries,

so that I can review cases faster.

**Story 2:**

As a medical reviewer,

I want risk explanations,

so that I can validate AI recommendations.

**Story 3:**

As a compliance officer,

I want audit history,

so that I can verify regulatory compliance.

**15. Acceptance Criteria Example**

**Story:**

AI Case Summary

Acceptance:

Given:

A valid report is uploaded.

When:

AI processing completes.

Then:

The system displays:

-   Patient information

-   Drug information

-   Event details

**16. Agile Execution Plan**

**Epic:**

AI Pharmacovigilance Platform

**Sprint 1:**

**Lesson 34 -- Pharmacovigilance System Analysis**

**Complete Safety System Workflow Analysis for a Healthcare BA**

**Module Progress Reminder**

Your roadmap:

**Total Lessons:** 36

Completed:

✅ Lesson 1--33

Current:

➡️ **Lesson 34 of 36**

**2 Lessons Left**

**1. Why Pharmacovigilance Domain Knowledge Matters for a BA?**

A general BA understands:

-   Requirements

-   Processes

-   Stakeholders

-   Systems

A **Healthcare BA** must additionally understand:

-   Medical workflows

-   Regulatory expectations

-   Safety processes

-   Industry terminology

Your advantage:

You already understand pharmacology, so you can connect:

**Clinical Domain → Business Need → Software Requirement**

**2. What is Pharmacovigilance (PV)?**

**Definition**

Pharmacovigilance is the science and activities related to detecting,
assessing, understanding, and preventing adverse effects or other
medicine-related problems.

Simple:

PV ensures:

\"Medicines remain safe after reaching patients.\"

**3. Pharmacovigilance System Overview**

A typical PV ecosystem:

Patient / Doctor / Hospital

↓

Adverse Event Report

↓

Safety Database

↓

Case Processing

↓

Medical Review

↓

Signal Detection

↓

Regulatory Reporting

**4. PV System Stakeholders**

**1. Patient**

Provides safety information.

Example:

\"I experienced nausea after taking Drug X.\"

**2. Healthcare Professional**

Reports adverse events.

**3. Drug Safety Associate**

Processes cases.

**4. Medical Reviewer**

Evaluates clinical significance.

**5. Pharmacovigilance Manager**

Ensures compliance.

**6. Regulatory Authority**

Receives safety reports.

Examples:

-   FDA

-   EMA

-   CDSCO

**5. Adverse Event Case Lifecycle**

This is very important for BA interviews.

Complete lifecycle:

Case Intake

↓

Case Creation

↓

Data Entry

↓

Medical Coding

↓

Assessment

↓

Quality Review

↓

Submission

↓

Follow-up

↓

Closure

Let\'s understand each stage.

**6. Stage 1 -- Case Intake**

**What happens?**

Safety information enters the system.

Sources:

-   Email

-   Call center

-   Website

-   Literature

-   Regulatory authority

BA Requirements:

System should support multiple intake channels.

Example Requirement:

FR-001:

\"The system shall allow users to create safety cases from incoming
reports.\"

**7. Stage 2 -- Case Creation**

A case record is created.

Minimum information:

**Four Minimum Criteria:**

1.  Identifiable patient

2.  Identifiable reporter

3.  Suspect product

4.  Adverse event

BA Business Rule:

System shall not allow case validation without minimum criteria.

**8. Stage 3 -- Data Entry**

Information captured:

**Patient Information**

-   Age

-   Gender

-   Medical history

**Drug Information**

-   Product name

-   Dose

-   Route

-   Batch number

**Event Information**

-   Symptoms

-   Seriousness

-   Outcome

BA Consideration:

Which fields are mandatory?

Which fields are optional?

**9. Stage 4 -- Medical Coding**

Raw medical terms are converted into standardized terminology.

Examples:

Patient says:

\"Heart attack\"

System coding:

\"Myocardial infarction\"

BA Requirements:

System should support controlled medical dictionaries.

**10. Stage 5 -- Medical Assessment**

Medical reviewer evaluates:

-   Seriousness

-   Severity

-   Causality

-   Expectedness

Example:

Question:

\"Is Drug X responsible for liver injury?\"

BA Requirement:

System shall allow medical reviewers to record assessment decisions.

**11. Stage 6 -- Quality Review**

Quality team checks:

-   Completeness

-   Accuracy

-   Compliance

BA Requirement:

System shall support reviewer approval workflow.

**12. Stage 7 -- Regulatory Submission**

Safety reports may be submitted to authorities.

Examples:

-   Individual Case Safety Reports (ICSR)

BA Requirement:

System shall generate regulatory-compliant reports.

**13. Stage 8 -- Follow-up**

Additional information may arrive.

Example:

Initial report:

Patient experienced reaction.

Follow-up:

Lab results received.

BA Requirement:

System shall maintain complete case history.

**14. Stage 9 -- Case Closure**

Case is completed.

System records:

-   Final assessment

-   Submission status

-   Approval history

**15. PV System Modules**

A modern PV platform may contain:

**1. Case Management Module**

Handles:

-   Case creation

-   Workflow

-   Tracking

**2. Document Management**

Stores:

-   Reports

-   Attachments

-   Evidence

**3. Medical Review Module**

Handles:

-   Assessment

-   Decisions

**4. Reporting Module**

Creates:

-   Regulatory reports

-   Dashboards

**5. Signal Detection Module**

Identifies:

Potential safety concerns.

**6. AI Assistance Module**

Provides:

-   Data extraction

-   Summary generation

-   Risk prediction

**16. BA Requirement Examples for PV System**

**Functional Requirements**

**FR-001**

System shall allow safety users to create adverse event cases.

**FR-002**

System shall maintain complete case history.

**FR-003**

System shall support medical assessment workflow.

**FR-004**

System shall generate regulatory reports.

**FR-005**

System shall maintain audit trails.

**17. Non-Functional Requirements**

**Security**

\"Only authorized users shall access patient safety information.\"

**Performance**

\"System shall load case details within 3 seconds.\"

**Auditability**

\"System shall record all case modifications.\"

**Availability**

\"System shall maintain 99.5% uptime.\"

**18. PV AI Use Cases**

Modern AI applications:

**1. Intelligent Case Intake**

AI extracts:

-   Drug names

-   Events

-   Patient details

**2. Duplicate Case Detection**

AI identifies:

Same patient + same event + same drug.

**3. Medical Summary Generation**

AI creates reviewer summaries.

**4. Risk Prioritization**

AI identifies:

High-risk cases.

**5. Literature Screening**

AI analyzes scientific publications.

**19. BA Process Modeling Example**

**AS-IS**

Report Received

↓

Manual Entry

↓

Manual Review

↓

Manual Priority Decision

↓

Submission

Problems:

-   Slow

-   Labour intensive

-   Error possibility

**TO-BE**

Report Received

↓

AI Extraction

↓

Automatic Case Creation

↓

AI Risk Score

↓

Human Validation

↓

Submission

Benefits:

-   Faster processing

-   Better consistency

-   Reduced workload

✅ Release Notes

**Lesson 35 -- Digital Health Product Requirements Masterclass**

**How a BA Analyzes and Defines Requirements for Digital Health
Products**

**Module Progress Reminder**

Your roadmap:

**Total Lessons:** 36

Completed:

✅ Lesson 1--34

Current:

➡️ **Lesson 35 of 36**

**1 Lesson Left**

**1. What is a Digital Health Product?**

**Definition**

A digital health product uses software, data, mobile technology, AI, or
connected devices to improve healthcare delivery, patient outcomes, or
clinical decision-making.

Examples:

-   Telemedicine platforms

-   Patient monitoring apps

-   Electronic health records

-   Medication reminder apps

-   AI diagnostic assistance

-   Remote patient monitoring systems

**2. Why Does a BA Need Digital Health Knowledge?**

A healthcare BA must understand:

-   Patient journey

-   Healthcare workflows

-   Clinical users

-   Regulatory requirements

-   Data privacy

-   Interoperability

A normal BA asks:

\"What feature does the user need?\"

A healthcare BA asks:

\"How does this improve healthcare outcomes safely?\"

**3. Digital Health Product Case Study**

We will design:

**AI Remote Patient Monitoring Platform**

**Product Vision:**

Enable doctors to monitor chronic disease patients remotely using
AI-powered health insights.

**4. Business Problem Analysis**

**Current Situation (AS-IS)**

Patients with chronic diseases:

-   Visit hospitals frequently

-   Monitoring happens only during appointments

-   Early warning signs may be missed

**Business Problems:**

**Problem 1:**

Doctors lack continuous patient information.

**Problem 2:**

Patients delay seeking help when symptoms worsen.

**Problem 3:**

Healthcare providers spend more time managing critical cases.

**5. Business Objective**

The organization wants to:

1.  Monitor patients continuously.

2.  Detect health risks early.

3.  Reduce avoidable hospital visits.

4.  Improve patient engagement.

**6. Stakeholder Analysis**

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Stakeholder**                **Need**
  ------------------------------ ----------------------------------------
  Patient                        Easy health monitoring

  Doctor                         Patient insights

  Nurse                          Follow-up management

  Hospital                       Better care efficiency

  IT Team                        Secure system

  Compliance Team                Regulatory adherence

  Product Manager                Product success
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**7. User Journey Mapping**

Very important BA skill.

**Patient Journey**

Patient Enrollment

↓

Connect Device

↓

Health Data Collection

↓

AI Analysis

↓

Receive Alerts

↓

Doctor Consultation

**Doctor Journey**

Login Dashboard

↓

View Patient Data

↓

Review AI Alerts

↓

Contact Patient

↓

Update Care Plan

**8. Functional Requirements**

Now BA converts needs into system requirements.

**FR-001**

**Patient Registration**

The system shall allow patients to create accounts.

Acceptance Criteria:

Given:

Patient enters valid information.

When:

Registration is submitted.

Then:

Account should be created.

**FR-002**

**Health Data Collection**

The system shall collect:

-   Heart rate

-   Blood pressure

-   Blood glucose

-   Activity data

**FR-003**

**AI Risk Prediction**

The system shall analyze patient data and generate risk alerts.

**FR-004**

**Doctor Dashboard**

The system shall allow doctors to view patient health trends.

**FR-005**

**Alert Management**

The system shall notify healthcare providers when abnormal values are
detected.

**9. Non-Functional Requirements**

Digital health products require strong NFRs.

**Security**

\"The system shall protect patient health information through encryption
and access control.\"

**Performance**

\"The dashboard shall load patient information within 3 seconds.\"

**Availability**

\"The platform shall maintain 99.9% availability.\"

**Scalability**

\"The system shall support 1 million patient records.\"

**Auditability**

\"The system shall maintain complete activity history.\"

**10. Healthcare UX Requirements**

Healthcare UX is different from normal apps.

BA must consider:

-   Elderly users

-   Low technical knowledge

-   Emergency situations

-   Accessibility

Example:

Bad Requirement:

\"Create dashboard.\"

Better:

\"Create a patient dashboard that displays critical health information
using simple visual indicators.\"

**11. Patient App Requirements**

Features:

**Health Dashboard**

Shows:

-   Current readings

-   Trends

-   Alerts

**Medication Reminder**

Allows:

-   Medication schedule

-   Notifications

-   Confirmation

**Doctor Communication**

Allows:

-   Secure messaging

-   Appointment requests

**12. Doctor Dashboard Requirements**

Doctor needs:

**Patient Overview**

Information:

-   Current condition

-   Risk level

-   Historical trends

**AI Insights**

Example:

\"Patient blood pressure trend increased by 20% in last 7 days.\"

**Action Management**

Doctor can:

-   Contact patient

-   Update care plan

-   Schedule follow-up

**13. API & Integration Requirements**

Digital health systems rarely work alone.

They integrate with:

-   Wearable devices

-   Hospital systems

-   Laboratory systems

-   Electronic Health Records

BA Requirement Example:

\"The system shall integrate with hospital EHR systems to retrieve
patient clinical information.\"

**14. Data Requirements**

Healthcare data includes:

**Patient Data**

-   Demographics

-   Medical history

**Clinical Data**

-   Diagnosis

-   Medication

-   Lab results

**Monitoring Data**

-   Vital signs

-   Activity data

BA must define:

-   Data source

-   Data owner

-   Data access rules

**15. AI Requirements**

AI features need special BA analysis.

**AI Prediction**

Requirement:

\"The system shall generate patient risk scores based on historical
health data.\"

**Explainability**

Requirement:

\"The system shall provide reasons behind generated alerts.\"

**Human Oversight**

Requirement:

\"AI-generated recommendations shall require healthcare professional
review.\"

**16. Agile Product Backlog Example**

**Epic:**

Remote Patient Monitoring

**Feature 1:**

Patient Health Tracking

User Story:

\"As a patient, I want to view my health metrics so that I can monitor
my condition.\"

**Feature 2:**

AI Health Alerts

User Story:

\"As a doctor, I want AI alerts so that I can identify high-risk
patients early.\"

**Feature 3:**

Doctor Dashboard

User Story:

\"As a physician, I want patient trends so that I can make informed
decisions.\"

**17. UAT Example**

**Scenario:**

AI Alert Generation

Steps:

1.  Patient data crosses threshold.

2.  AI analyzes information.

3.  Alert generated.

Expected:

Doctor receives notification.

✅ Process Flow

✅ BRD\

**Delivery**

✅ Product Backlog\

✅ Release Notes

**19. Interview Answer Example**

Question:

\"How do you gather requirements for a healthcare product?\"

Answer:

\"I start by understanding the healthcare workflow, identifying
stakeholders such as patients, clinicians, and compliance teams. I
analyze current processes, identify pain points, define business
requirements, convert them into functional and non-functional
requirements, and validate them through user stories and acceptance
criteria.\"

**Final Project**

**AI Pharmacovigilance Intelligence Platform**

**Complete BA Portfolio Case Study**

**1. Project Overview**

**Project Name:**

AI Pharmacovigilance Intelligence Platform

**Industry:**

Pharmaceutical / Life Sciences

**Project Type:**

AI-enabled Drug Safety Management System

**BA Role:**

Business Analyst / Product Analyst

**2. Business Background**

A pharmaceutical company receives thousands of adverse event reports
every month.

Current process:

-   Manual report review

-   Manual data extraction

-   Manual risk assessment

-   Manual prioritization

This creates:

-   Processing delays

-   High operational cost

-   Reviewer workload

-   Compliance risks

**3. Business Problem Statement**

**Current Situation**

Safety teams manually analyze adverse event reports from multiple
sources.

**Problem**

The existing process cannot efficiently handle increasing case volume.

**Business Impact**

-   Longer review cycles

-   Delayed safety decisions

-   Increased operational effort

**Business Need**

Build an AI-assisted pharmacovigilance platform that improves case
processing efficiency while maintaining human oversight.

**4. Stakeholder Analysis**

**Stakeholder Matrix**

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Stakeholder**           **Role**           **Interest**
  ------------------------- ------------------ --------------------------
  VP Pharmacovigilance      Sponsor            Business outcome

  Safety Manager            Product Owner      Workflow efficiency

  Safety Reviewer           End User           Faster review

  Medical Reviewer          Clinical Validator Accuracy

  Compliance Team           Regulator          Audit readiness

  IT Team                   Delivery           System implementation

  Data Science Team         AI Model           AI capability
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**5. Business Objectives**

**Objective 1**

Reduce manual case review time by 50%.

**Objective 2**

Improve identification of high-risk cases.

**Objective 3**

Maintain regulatory compliance.

**Objective 4**

Provide complete audit transparency.

**6. Scope Definition**

**In Scope**

✅ Case intake management\
✅ AI document extraction\
✅ AI case summary\
✅ Risk classification\
✅ Reviewer dashboard\
✅ Audit trail\
✅ Regulatory reporting support

**Out of Scope**

❌ Drug discovery\
❌ Clinical diagnosis\
❌ Treatment recommendation

**7. AS-IS Process Analysis**

Current workflow:

Report Received

↓

Manual Data Entry

↓

Safety Review

↓

Medical Assessment

↓

Regulatory Reporting

**Problems Identified**

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Process Step**                    **Issue**
  ----------------------------------- -----------------------------------
  Intake                              Multiple sources

  Data Entry                          Manual effort

  Review                              Time consuming

  Risk Assessment                     Inconsistent

  Reporting                           Delayed
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**8. TO-BE Process Design**

Future workflow:

Report Received

↓

AI Data Extraction

↓

Automatic Case Creation

↓

AI Summary Generation

↓

AI Risk Classification

↓

Human Validation

↓

Regulatory Submission

**9. BPMN Workflow Concept**

**Actors:**

-   Reporter

-   AI Engine

-   Safety Reviewer

-   Medical Reviewer

-   Compliance Team

Process:

Start

↓

Receive Safety Report

↓

Validate Minimum Criteria

↓

AI Extract Information

↓

Generate Summary

↓

Risk Assessment

↓

Reviewer Approval

↓

Submit Report

↓

End

**10. Business Requirements (BRD)**

**BR-001**

The organization requires an automated system to improve adverse event
processing efficiency.

**BR-002**

The system should reduce manual review effort.

**BR-003**

The system must maintain regulatory compliance.

**BR-004**

The system should provide AI-assisted decision support.

**11. Functional Requirements (FRD)**

**FR-001: Case Creation**

The system shall allow users to create adverse event cases.

Acceptance:

Given a valid report,

When submitted,

Then a case record should be created.

**FR-002: AI Information Extraction**

The system shall extract:

-   Drug information

-   Patient information

-   Medical events

**FR-003: AI Summary Generation**

The system shall generate case summaries.

**FR-004: Risk Classification**

The system shall classify cases based on risk level.

**FR-005: Audit Trail**

The system shall record:

-   User

-   Action

-   Timestamp

-   Changes

**12. Non-Functional Requirements**

**Performance**

System should generate AI summaries within 30 seconds.

**Security**

Patient data must be encrypted.

**Availability**

System should maintain 99.5% availability.

**Scalability**

System should support increasing case volume.

**Explainability**

AI recommendations should include reasoning.

**13. User Stories**

**Epic:**

AI-Assisted Safety Review

**User Story 1**

As a safety reviewer,

I want AI-generated summaries,

so that I can review cases faster.

Acceptance Criteria:

Given:

A report is uploaded.

When:

AI processing completes.

Then:

Summary should display key information.

**User Story 2**

As a medical reviewer,

I want AI risk explanations,

so that I can validate safety decisions.

Acceptance Criteria:

Risk score and explanation should be visible.

**User Story 3**

As a compliance officer,

I want audit history,

so that I can verify regulatory compliance.

Acceptance Criteria:

All user activities should be logged.

**14. Product Backlog Example**

  -----------------------------------------------------------------------
  **Priority**            **Item**
  ----------------------- -----------------------------------------------
  High                    Case Intake

  High                    AI Extraction

  High                    Risk Classification

  Medium                  Dashboard Filters

  Medium                  Reporting

  Low                     Custom Themes
  -----------------------------------------------------------------------

**Sprint 1:**

AI Risk Classification

Steps:

1.  Upload adverse event report.

2.  Run AI analysis.

3.  Review risk result.

Expected:

System displays:

-   Risk category

-   Confidence score

-   Explanation

**17. Defect Example**

**Defect:**

AI incorrectly classifies serious adverse event.

Severity:

Critical

Priority:

High

Business Impact:

Potential safety risk.
